# Preface

## To the Impartial Reader.

Kind reader, this German discourse—which has been compiled by a well-practiced and long-experienced chemist <term>Chymico; a term for an alchemist or chemical philosopher</term> and observed through many-fold practical work <note>original: *laboriren*; refers specifically to laboratory experimentation</note>—is given and presented to you for your benefit and piety, provided you yourself have a desire for the truth. It is not that the author, who loves God and the truth, seeks any worldly fame or a great name through it; no, his heart, which has been well known to me for several years now, shows otherwise. Nor is it that he wishes to bring great profit and wealth to himself through the publication of such a secret <term>Arcano; a hidden or mysterious alchemical procedure</term>, which is unfairly criticized by almost all the inexperienced and unlearned. Instead, his goal is primarily directed toward the glory of God and, as mentioned above, the benefit of his neighbor.

Although the aforementioned author, in verbal colleges <note>original: *Collegiis verbalibus*; refers to traditional university settings where learning was based on lecturing and rhetoric rather than manual experimentation</note>, and where one only [knows] much of Aris-

<summary>This preface introduces the text as a practical manual written by an experienced alchemist who prioritizes God's glory and the reader's benefit over personal fame or wealth. It contrasts the author's hands-on experimental knowledge with the theoretical "verbal" education of traditional universities.</summary>

<keywords>preface, chemist, alchemist, practical experimentation, Arcanum, divine glory, Aristotle, education, scholasticism</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the preface, specifically completing a sentence from the previous page that criticized those who rely solely on "Aristotelian" academic theories without practical experience. The catchword "stote-" from the previous page connects to "stotelischen" here.</meta>

totelian whims <note>original: *stotelischen Grillen*; refers to the speculative or overly theoretical ideas associated with scholastic followers of Aristotle</note> is unknown / for **it is better to live for God and be unknown to the world, than to live for the world and be unknown to God** <note>original: *melius est vivere Deo & ignotum esse mundo, quàm vivere mundo, & ignotum esse Deo*</note>:

Yet I know this: he will reveal himself to true philosophers as a **legitimate son of doctrine** <note>original: *legitimum Doctrinæ filium*</note>, whom God has willed to lead to the truth through His almighty hand of grace, miraculously through rocks, through fires, through a thousand dangers <note>original: *per Saxa, per ignes, per mille pericula*</note>. And indeed, it does not require much commendation; the work praises the master himself. He has not compiled such a work merely from imaginings or from **rational deductions** <note>original: *vernünfftigen consequentiis*</note>—which many a man spins and ponders **in his own little brain** <note>original: *in suo cerebello*</note> based on the writings of ancient naturalists and true **followers of Hermes** <term>Mercurialischen; those dedicated to the Hermetic or "mercurial" arts of alchemy</term>—but has instead handled the coals himself, built his own furnaces, observed the **degrees of fire** <note>original: *gradus ignis*</note>, and made himself well-versed in his noble **art of assaying** <term>Probierkunst; the technical skill of testing and refining ores and metals to determine their purity</term> for the knowledge of every mineral. 

Until finally, after many recounted errors, through the heartfelt mercy of God, he reached the right **goal** <note>original: *Scopo*</note> and the Bride for whom he had so long wooed. Previously, instead of Rachel, he always had to deal with the cross-eyed Leah <note>A biblical allegory for the alchemist's struggle: Jacob worked seven years for Rachel but was tricked into marrying Leah first. Here, Leah represents the frustrating, "lackluster" results of failed experiments that precede the "beautiful" discovery of the truth (Rachel)</note>. And I must confess that in all my life I have never seen a clearer writing, for it seems as if it were a **compendium of the whole truth of philosophy, sincerely extracted from all sincere philosophers** <note>original: *Compendium totius veritatis philosophiæ, ex omnibus sinceris philosophis sincerè extractum*</note> and **proven by the very deed** <note>original: *& ipso facto comprobatum*</note>. Therefore, the reading of it never—

<summary>The author argues that true alchemical knowledge comes from God and grueling physical labor—handling coals and building furnaces—rather than academic theory. He uses the biblical story of Jacob, Rachel, and Leah to illustrate the long, deceptive path of trial and error that eventually leads to the "bride" of alchemical truth.</summary>

<keywords>Aristotelianism vs Practice, degrees of fire, assaying, Rachel and Leah allegory, Hermetic philosophy, experimental proof, divine grace</keywords>

->To the Impartial Reader.<-

[no one] will regret it. But if one is filled with other unfounded opinions <note>original: *opinionibus*</note>, then even the bright light of truth will seem like darkness to him; especially since GOD—whose secret counsel is an "Unground" and a depth <note>original: *Ungrund und Tieffe*; "Ungrund" is a mystical term used by thinkers like Jacob Böhme to describe the primal, unfathomable void of God's essence before creation</note>—does not yet wish to make our darkness light. Perhaps, because of the world’s great ungratefulness, it is not yet the time for such great wisdom to become common—wisdom whose ruler and possessor is GOD the Holy Spirit alone, who is sevenfold, indeed thousand-fold in His gift <note>original: *qui septiformis, imò milleformis munere*</note>.

But for those who are in Christ Jesus, and whose conduct is consistent and oriented toward heaven—that is, toward the right, true body of which Christ is the head—and who have not yet drunk from the self-dug cisterns of false philosophers, nor from the Babylonian Antichristian poison cup, but instead sigh from the heart for the blood of the Lamb and its prototype, the <term>universal tincture</term> <note>the "universal tincture" or "philosopher's stone" was considered by Christian alchemists to be a physical manifestation of Christ's healing power, capable of perfecting both metals and the human soul</note>: to those, may GOD (who alone is wise) open and enlighten their hearts, minds, and thoughts alongside mine. May we all be of one mind, loving one another and seeking not our own interests, but the honor of GOD and the service of our neighbor, just as this author has done. May we love Him, searching for and recognizing His glory, omnipotence, and love within Nature—which is the true Divine power—and thus, surrendered <note>original: *gelassen*; a term for "Gelassenheit" or spiritual yieldedness, a central concept in German mysticism</note> to Him, we may one day with all the elect and the Seraphim...

## THE AUTHOR’S
# Preface.

<image-desc>A large ornamental drop-cap 'E' featuring scrolling foliage and vines begins the first word of the text.</image-desc>

Beloved and favorable reader, many might wonder at this German discourse, especially among those who know my person and my name, as to why I have undertaken to write so clearly and distinctly about such a high secret <note>original: "arcano"; a hidden mystery of nature or a specialized alchemical preparation</note>, since otherwise the philosophers <note>original: "Philosophi"; in this context, the "philosophers" are the master alchemists of the past</note> commonly kept their mouths closed to the highest degree. Usually, their writings were only published <note>original: "publiciret"</note> after their deaths, and even then, not purely and clearly, but according to their custom in the greatest obscurity, leaving out the most essential part or otherwise tying a hard knot, so that almost no one dared to draw any foundation <note>original: "Fundament"</note> from them. Thus, I must indeed confess myself that because I have written so clearly according to the old view <note>original: "Meynung"</note>, many will worry about this and about my person, thinking it to be a compiled opinion <note>original: "opinion"</note> and never personally tested <note>original: "experimentirt"</note> by me; assuming that I am a poor fellow, not knowledgeable in languages, who has moreover discarded many fine things and, as it were, [thrown] everything like a mash into a po- <note>The sentence is interrupted by the end of the page; the catchword "fen" indicates the word is "Hafen," meaning a pot or vessel.</note>

<summary>In this preface, the author justifies his decision to reveal alchemical secrets in plain German, contrasting his clarity with the intentional obscurity of past "philosophers." He anticipates that critics may doubt his practical experience because he lacks formal education in classical languages.</summary>

<keywords>preface, alchemical secrets, plain language, experimental knowledge, obscurity vs clarity, philosophers</keywords>

But to this I answer briefly: that throughout my life I never entertained the thought of treating anything regarding this glorious and highly-praiseworthy <term>science</term> <note>original: "Scientz"; here referring to the "sacred science" of alchemy</note>, because I was always horrified whenever I read such books and could find no <term>aim</term> <note>original: "Scopum"; a clear target or guiding principle</note>. Therefore, I almost despaired that the fast-locked gates would be opened to me, the veil of ignorance removed <note>original: "das Fell der Unwissenheit benommen"; literally "the pelt of ignorance taken away," a metaphor for gaining spiritual or intellectual sight</note>, and that I should be admitted into the inner chamber of the secrets of nature; because alongside all my sharp and deep-thinking <term>contemplations</term> <note>original: "contemplationibus"</note>, I was never able to find or perceive any useful possibility within reach. For that reason, I held <term>alchemy</term> <note>original: "Chymiam"</note> in such high contempt in my heart that I intended to <term>bid it farewell</term> <note>original: "valediciren"</note> entirely.

And although in my <term>profession</term> <note>original: "Profession"</note> I gradually learned to recognize various <term>minerals</term> <note>original: "Mineralia"</note> and <term>metals</term> <note>original: "Metalla"</note>—though not without serious reflection—these <term>instigated</term> <note>original: "instigireten"</note> me to <term>speculate</term> <note>original: "speculiren"</note> anew and brought me to the point where, with a firm resolution and Divine help, I desired to <term>observe</term> <note>original: "observiren"</note> the <term>agreements of the philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophorum concordantien"; the points of consensus among historical alchemical masters</note>. Alongside this, I well-considered the many known errors of myself and other people, and finally became so firm in my <term>theory</term> <note>original: "Theoria"</note> that I believed if it could not be <term>put into practice</term> <note>original: "practiciren"</note>, the <term>stone</term> <note>original: "lapis"; the Philosopher's Stone</note> would never be made in all eternity. It may happen, beloved and favorable reader, that many will wonder about this German discourse—especially those who know my person and name—as to why I have undertaken to write so clearly and distinctly about such a high <term>secret</term> <note>original: "arcano"; a hidden mystery of nature or alchemy</note>. For usually, the <term>philosophers</term> <note>original: "Philosophi"; the historical masters of alchemy</note> kept their mouths tightly closed, and only after their death were their writings published; even then, they were not plain and clear, but according to their custom, were published in the deepest obscurity. The most essential points were left out, or else a difficult knot was tied, so that almost no one dared to derive any <term>foundation</term> <note>original: "Fundament"; a basic principle or starting point</note> from them.

Thus, I must indeed confess that because I have written so clearly according to the old tradition, many will be troubled by this and by my person. They will think it is merely a gathered <term>opinion</term> <note>original: "opinion"</note> and was never <term>experimented</term> <note>original: "experimentirt"; tested through practical laboratory work</note> by me. They may assume that I am but a poor fellow, unskilled in languages, who has moreover discarded many fine things and thrown everything together like a mash into a single pot—

## The Author’s Preface.

[Out of] <note>The catchword from the previous page was "Con-", which completes the word "Condolentz"</note> **lamentation** <note>original: *Condolentz*; here meaning a sorrowful sympathy or regret for the state of the world</note> for the overabundant **deceit** / added to which / I hope / that no one will complain or look askance at this my kind work / but rather recognize / that I have not hidden the mercy I obtained—writing so clearly as if such a thing had not happened to me—but have felt no shame in revealing it with glorious praise; not intending to falsely promote myself / or to seek a **stinking honor** <note>a common 17th-century idiom for fleeting, worldly, or ill-gotten fame</note> / for **GOD** the Most High is my only ornament and honor / much less to desire money and property / which I could otherwise easily have obtained through useless **communication** <note>original: *Communication*; referring to the sharing of alchemical secrets for profit</note>: No, by no means: only so / that **GOD’S** works may be revealed and his holy name may be praised. For the rest, I have enough treasure in my heart / and let myself be satisfied with little.

## The Author’s Preface.

Whoever lets themselves be fooled / let them know / that I, along with Bernard <note>original: *Bernhard*; likely referring to Bernard of Trevisan (1406–1490), a famous alchemist whose works emphasized the "keys" to the secret art</note>, have vowed to God and Nature / that I will stop completely with this little treatise / and let a lock be pressed upon my mouth from now on / just as I wish to refer everyone to the certain assurance / that no one / whoever they may be / shall obtain anything more from me beyond this publication / neither through bribes nor threats. And because I certainly assure everyone in abundance / that I have omitted nothing / which is necessary for this **work** <term>work</term> <note>original: *Werck*; the "Great Work" (*Magnum Opus*) of alchemy, the process of creating the Philosopher's Stone</note> / let him be faithfully commanded / that he diligently repeat everything read / just like the pure ruminating animals <note>An allusion to the biblical "clean" animals that chew the cud; a common early modern metaphor for deep, repetitive study and meditation on difficult texts</note> / so that he may gradually grasp one thing and another.

Regarding my humble person / if anyone should wish to take offense / it is at their pleasure / for I hold / that the **subject** <note>original: *Subjectum*</note> itself is a **stumbling block** <note>original: *Stein der Ergernis*; a biblical reference to something that causes people to trip or take offense, often used regarding spiritual truths</note> for everyone / it matters little / whether one **judges** <note>original: *judiciret*</note> it white or black / and even if my writings seem strange to the heads of **Master Smart-alecks** <note>original: *Meister Klüglings Köpff*; a mocking term for those who think they are clever but lack true wisdom</note> / they will nevertheless have to let them stand upon the foundation of truth. But as for my keeping my name somewhat hidden / let no one be suspicious / as if I were shunning the light for that reason / but rather that this is done more upon the advice of distinguished people / and it would be spoken ill of me as an upright Evangelical **cross- and burden-bearer** of Christ / now / I who previously

## The Author’s Preface.

...and let myself be driven and rubbed by many a dark wind / and suffered all kinds of misery / not to hide myself according to old German integrity <note>original: *Teutscher Redlichkeit*; the author appeals to a perceived national character of honesty and plain-dealing</note> / but only merely to experience with what kind of spirit the **great works of God** <term>great works of God</term> <note>original: *magnalia Dei*</note> will be accepted by the current ruling evil world.

Meanwhile / as one or another knows me / or learns my name from my **writing** <term>writing</term> <note>original: *Scripto*</note> / he will undoubtedly / as an honor-loving **philosopher** <term>philosopher</term> <note>original: *Philosophus*</note> and a true **Nicodemite** <term>Nicodemite</term> <note>original: *Nicodemit*; a term for someone who hides their true beliefs to avoid persecution, named after the biblical figure Nicodemus who visited Jesus in secret at night</note> / hide me out of unfeigned love / and love my **fellowship** <term>fellowship</term> <note>original: *Societatem*</note> in secret / for which he, in turn, shall hold himself assured / that he will have to expect the fame of it before everyone / and also all gratitude from me upon the occurring **opportunity** <term>opportunity</term> <note>original: *occasion*</note>.

Thus, alongside the recommendation of Divine **protection** <term>protection</term> <note>original: *protection*</note>, I have hereby wished to heartfully and brotherly announce this to every faithful **son of Hermes** <term>son of Hermes</term> <note>original: *Filio Hermetis*; a traditional title for an initiate into the alchemical or "Hermetic" secrets</note> / etc.

# TO GOD ALONE
## THE GLORY,

When I consider how very many noble and learned people have written of this high **secret** <term>secret</term> <note>original: *Secreto*; referring here to the "Great Secret" of alchemy or the composition of the Philosopher's Stone</note> / I must be almost terrified / that so few of them **concord** <term>concord</term> <note>original: *concordiren*</note> and agree with one another / for whoever reads their writings / finds / that one is of this / the other of that opinion / one makes a dry / the other a wet **stone** <term>stone</term> <note>original: *Stein*; the Philosopher's Stone, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold</note> / this one wants to have the **air** <term>air</term> <note>original: *Lufft*</note> / that one the **water** <term>water</term> <note>original: *Wasser*</note> / one says / such a noble **little pearl** <term>little pearl</term> <note>original: *Pernlein*; a metaphorical term for the essential "seed" or quintessence of nature</note> is in all things / the other says No to it.

Although there are many nowadays / who intend to maintain their **opinions** <term>opinions</term> <note>original: *opiniones*</note> or writings with stately **arguments** <term>arguments</term> <note>original: *argumentis*</note> / and to **defend** <term>defend</term> <note>original: *defendiren*</note> them with philosophical sayings / experience yet shows /

<summary>The author begins Part One by expressing his dismay at the lack of consensus among alchemical writers, noting that their descriptions of the "Stone" and its elements are often contradictory. He observes that while many authors use sophisticated arguments to defend their views, practical experience often tells a different story.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, Philosopher's Stone, contradiction, secret, elements, experience vs theory</keywords>

Who is to be found today who can truly say he has become rich through transmutation <note>The alchemical process of changing base metals, like lead, into noble metals like gold or silver</note>? Which physician <note>original: *Medicus*</note> can boast that he has discovered the means to quickly cure all diseases? Rather, one finds those who complain with deep sighs that they have sought for many years in a row, labored <note>original: *Laboriret*; specifically refers to practical laboratory work with furnaces and vessels</note> without ceasing, and spent all they owned, yet have seen nothing certain or even the slightest thing in which they could find comfort, either in medicine or transmutation.

Since this is such a loathsome matter—which makes the rich poor, the clever into fools, and the respected into the most despised—who would not judge <note>original: *judiciren*</note> along with the common man that all chemical books <note>original: *Chymische Bücher*</note> should be burned, and that such people, according to the old written laws, should not be tolerated in any community? Furthermore, that one should no longer hold the slightest regard for such things, especially since not only private individuals <note>original: *Privat-Personen*</note> lose their property through them, but even Princes and Lords are most highly deceived and betrayed.

I myself, through the special testing of God, was lost and confused in this art as if in a **labyrinth** for twenty-two years, so that I often despaired that not a single way or means could be found that led to the desired end—to pluck the fruits of the **Hermetic Tree** <note>A symbolic representation of the alchemical process; its fruits represent the completed Philosopher's Stone</note>. B 4

first, I shall take up the **partial transmutations of iron** <note>original: *Martis Particularia*; "Mars" is the alchemical name for iron, and "particularia" refers to processes intended to produce small amounts of gold or silver rather than the universal "Philosopher's Stone"</note> and make them known to the lover of the Art, so that he might not be seduced by the manifold **processes** <note>original: *Process*</note> with which almost all books are filled, and which are shouted out by **charlatans** <note>original: *Jaganten*; likely a derivative of "giants," used here to mean boastful or arrogant deceivers</note> as certain **transmutations**. Rather, through my sincere warning, may he free himself from the fraud and escape the cunning deception while preserving both his wealth and his spirit.

The enticement of such fraudsters toward the worthless yields of **iron** <note>original: *Martis*</note> begins with this lovely pipe-song: they say that iron possesses a superfluous and very red, fixed **Sulphur** <note>original: *Sulphur*; in alchemy, this refers to the "soul" or coloring principle of a metal</note>, which far surpasses the **Sulphur of Gold** <note>original: *Sulphur Solis*</note> because of its deep redness and staining power. Therefore, they say one must only see how such a substance might gain **entry** <note>original: *Ingreß*; the ability of an alchemical substance to penetrate and fuse with another metal</note>; then it would unfailingly stain **silver** <note>original: *Lunam*; literally "the Moon"</note>—which supposedly lacks nothing else but such a fixed Sulphur—into permanent **gold** <note>original: *aurum*</note>, either **partially** <note>original: *Particulariter*</note> or **totally** <note>original: *totaliter*</note>. For this reason, the **Philosophers** <note>The traditional term for alchemists and natural scientists</note> have attributed the crown of all partial transmutations to the Sulphur of iron. To recount all of this in detail would be too lengthy, and besides, it is already more well-known to everyone than they might wish.

This is such a cruel, seductive, and false path that I consider it one of the worst next to the errors of **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio*</note>. One’s ears itch intensely when one finds such a beautiful **process** <note>original: *Process*</note> before them, which seemingly can be **worked** <note>original: *Elaboriren*; to process or refine in the laboratory</note> with little cost, which—

<summary>The author warns readers against deceptive alchemical texts that claim iron (Mars) contains a red sulphur capable of transforming silver into gold. He characterizes these "particular" processes as expensive, seductive traps that lead to financial and spiritual ruin.</summary>

# First Part

It seems entirely reasonable and promises a stately profit. Should one not be fooled when he sees how iron is such a cheap thing, and yet in **solution** <note>original: *Solution*; the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid</note> it appears like the most beautiful gold? Is it not so **fixed** <note>original: *fix*; in alchemy, a substance that is stable in fire and does not evaporate</note> in the fire that it is not consumed, but rather **increases**? Understand this to refer to the **Saffron of Mars** <note>original: *Croco Martis*; a red powder, usually iron oxide, created by calcining iron</note>. Does **iron** <note>original: *Mars*</note> not allow itself to be handled however one wishes? How easily (in everyone’s opinion) can it be transformed into copper? How does it, when smelted with **antimony** <note>original: *Antimonio*; a semi-metal used in the purification of gold</note>, yield such a beautiful, pure **regulus** <note>original: *Regulum*; the metallic button or "little king" found at the bottom of a crucible after smelting</note>, which not only looks like a fine silver, but nature even marks it with a beautiful **star** <note>the "Star Regulus of Antimony" is a crystalline pattern that forms on the surface of the metal, often interpreted by alchemists as a sign of divine favor or high purity</note>? How quickly it allows itself to be **transmuted** <note>original: *transmutiren*; to change from one substance into another</note> into its first essence, namely into a **sweet vitriol** <note>original: *Victriol*; iron sulfate, which forms green crystals</note>, from which afterward the permanent, ruby-red, incombustible **oil** <note>the "Oil of Mars" was a highly sought-after alchemical secret, believed to have great medicinal and transformative power</note> is to be brought forth?

Such **qualities** <note>original: *Qualitäten*</note> of iron can make the mouth water not only for a beginning student, but even for an old **Doctor**, so that he is seduced by it and no longer knows how to find his way back. I have seen for myself how the old **chemists** <note>original: *Chymici*</note> were so drowned in **iron** <note>original: *Martem*</note> and placed their trust in it to such an extent that they dared to **affirm the possibilities** <note>original: *poſſibilitäten affir-miren*</note> even at the risk of their own lives. But dear God, how different things are found to be in **experience** <note>original: *Experientz*</note>; how all hopes are in vain, and how it is with thi—

# of the Chemical Discourse.

...with this predatory, roguish fellow <note>referring back to iron or the false alchemist's materials described on the previous page</note>, nothing at all can be accomplished; and among all the recounted virtues, can none be brought to such an effect as one had hoped for?

Although a very beautiful **solution** <note>the liquid resulting from dissolving a solid</note> can be produced from iron—primarily from the **mineral of Mars** <note>original: *Minera Martis*; iron ore</note>—using **royal water** <note>original: *Aqua Regis*; a highly corrosive mixture of acids capable of dissolving even gold</note>, which in color closely resembles the solution of gold, it does not at all match the substance or the **fixity** <note>the quality of being "fixed" or stable in fire without evaporating</note> of gold. For after the **solution of Mars** <note>original: *Solutio Martis*</note> has **coagulated** <term>Coaguliret</term> <note>the process of a liquid thickening or solidifying into a solid mass</note>, it only becomes a white vitriol. This, upon being heated to a glowing heat, turns into a red **crocus** <note>original: *Croco*; a fine powder produced by calcination (roasting), in this case, iron oxide</note> and loses much of its weight. 

This **crocus** is no longer of a metallic nature, because the **magnet** no longer attracts it; nor can it be reduced back into a **body** <note>original: *Corpus*; the solid, metallic state</note>. Instead, it remains a harsh, vitriolic substance that has no **ingress** <note>original: *Ingreß*; the ability of an alchemical substance to penetrate and merge with another metal</note> and cannot be fundamentally joined with **silver** <note>original: *Lunæ*; literally "of the Moon," the alchemical name for silver</note> by any means or flux. It merely turns into a plain black slag. 

And even if, according to the opinion of many, this **crocus** or another were **sublimated** <note>the process of heating a substance into a vapor that then solidifies directly back into a powder</note>, resolved, coagulated, or even fixed with **sublimated mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio sublimato*; mercuric chloride</note> and **sal ammoniac** <note>original: *Salmiac*; ammonium chloride, often used as a flux to help metals melt</note> to the best of one's ability until the world’s end, no **transmutation of silver into gold** <note>original: *transmutatio Lunæ in Solem*</note> could be achieved with it. The reason is that the **sulfur of iron** <note>original: *Sulphur Martis*; alchemists believed metals were composed of a "sulfur" (soul/fire) and a "mercury" (spirit/liquidity)</note> is not the **sulfur of gold** <note>original: *Sulphur Solis*</note>, just as oat-flour is not wheat-flour. And in the event that some of it actually entered the **silver**, what use would it be? Would it not, because of the corrosive **salts** <note>original: *Salien*</note>, be...

# First Part

...noticeably diminished, completely brittle, and useless; so that, if the **bath of Saturn** <note>original: *Saturni Bad*; a purification process using molten lead to separate noble metals from impurities</note> were not available, through which the **noble body** <note>original: *Corpus*; likely referring to silver or gold</note> could purge itself of such a nasty, stinking **war-throat** <note>original: *Kriegsgurgel*; a derogatory alchemical nickname for iron, referencing Mars as the god of war</note>, one might otherwise be led to believe she was a mistress like **Venus** <note>the alchemical name for copper, often associated with iron in "marriages" of metals</note>. But after the bath, one sees how she does not wish to have the slightest love or fellowship with him; for she retains neither sweat, clothing, blood, property, nor the least bit of him. Consequently, in that **separation**, not a single grain of gold can be produced from a whole **mark** <note>a traditional unit of weight for precious metals</note> of silver.

And although many, even the cleverest and oldest **chemists** <note>original: *Chymici*</note>, claim otherwise, they deceive themselves to the highest degree. This happens because they perhaps used unrefined silver in their work, or because the **ore of Mars** <note>original: *Minera Martis*; iron ore</note> carried **gold pyrites** <term>Goldkiß</term> <note>often called "fool's gold," though it can sometimes contain trace amounts of actual gold</note> with it, as often happens, or the **ingredients** were already mixed with gold. How can this substance, which cannot even keep itself stable against any **element**, bring silver to such a property? ### of the Chemical Discourse.

...to help others; by which single saying all **particular operations** <note>original: *Particularia*; specific chemical recipes as opposed to the "Universal" work</note> and **processes** <note>original: *Process*</note> that do not originate from the true **foundation** and **center** are truly overthrown and rendered entirely worthless, no matter what one may say about the salt of iron and its sulfur. Any being that cannot sustain itself is much less able to sustain another; thus the **sulfur of gold** <note>original: *Sulphur Solis*</note> is one thing, and the **sulfur of iron** <note>original: *Sulphur Martis*</note> is another. As already mentioned, many have placed great stock in the **Star of Signature** <term>Signatstern</term> <note>A star-like crystalline pattern that forms on the surface of antimony when it is purified; alchemists viewed this as a "signature" or divine mark of the material's potential</note> and believed it to be nothing less than the purest **mercury of iron** <note>original: *Mercurius Martis*</note>, which they think is particularly comparable to the **mercury of gold and silver** <note>original: *Mercurio Solis & Lunæ*</note> and could easily be **solidified** <note>original: *coaguliren*</note> or **matured** <note>original: *digeriren*</note> into either gold or silver. This is supposedly proven by many thousands of processes that are meant to be **fixed** <note>original: *figiret*; the process of making a volatile substance resistant to fire</note> by this or that means; but to speak briefly, it is all futile labor. Effort, time, and expense are lost because the **star-regulus** <note>original: *Regulus Stellatus*; a metallic form of antimony produced by reducing the ore with iron</note> has been completely misunderstood.

To be sure, the **alchemists** believe that the **regulus of iron** <note>original: *Regulus Martis*</note> is separated from its superfluous sulfur with the help of **antimony**, so that the purest substance of iron joins with the **regulus**. But they are mistaken; if they take **equal parts** <note>original: *partes æquales*</note> of antimony and iron, they would find their regulus no purer, but rather completely disappeared, and in a

...slag <note>original: *Schlacken*</note> is found, such that neither **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> nor **regulus** <term>regulus</term> <note>The metallic button or mass that settles at the bottom of a crucible after smelting ore</note> can be detected anymore, and many a person becomes a fool over it. Finally, he had to confess with weeping eyes that this procedure had been his own speculation; he had placed such trust in it because the philosophers unanimously testified that the **sulfur of iron and antimony** <note>original: *Sulphur Martis & Antimonij*</note> was truly the means to profit **specifically** <note>original: *particulariter*</note> from **silver** <note>original: *Luna*</note>.

...very beautifully defended based on the fundamentals of the philosophers / and thus brought it about / that the Prince residing there / who was otherwise well-read / believed him / and lived in the hope / that if such slag <note>original: *Schlacken*; the stony waste matter separated from metals during smelting</note> remained in a **continuous flux** <note>original: *stetwerigen Fluß*; kept in a molten, liquid state in a furnace</note> for twelve years / the **English Tincture** <term>English Tincture</term> <note>A legendary alchemical preparation, often described as a "Universal Medicine" or a potent substance for transmuting base metals into gold</note> would inevitably be achieved.

There it was found / that through the six-year continuous flux the slag had not changed in the slightest / but slag had remained slag / and when some of it was applied to silver / it did not show the slightest possibility of success. Instead, one perceived / that there was nothing more to hope for / even if it had stood there for a hundred years.

...to be attributed to the excellent <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>original: *lapidi Philosophico*; the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold.</note> / and to further reinforce such claims / they invent and portray for people excellent <term>Partial Transmutations</term> <note>original: *Particular-Transmutationes*; chemical processes that supposedly change a small portion of metal into gold or silver, rather than the universal transformation promised by the "Great Work."</note> / as if the <term>Spirit of Venus</term> <note>original: *Spiritus Veneris*; a spirit or acid derived from copper.</note> could break down <term>Mars</term> <note>The alchemical name for **Iron**.</note> / which, after the <term>Conjunction</term> <note>original: *Conjunction*; the chemical union or "marriage" of two substances.</note>, would yield a blood-red oil / and could warm the cold bodies of silver, lead, and tin / and put gold in their place ; Likewise, that the <term>Oil</term> with its astringent <term>Salt</term> <note>original: *Sal*</note> eats away the wings of <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurio*; the alchemical name for **Quicksilver**. # First Part

...that you have a proven **emetic** <term>original: *Vomitiv*; a substance that induces vomiting</term> one way or the other; nature cannot be mastered by you in this **step** <note>original: *passu*; a Latin term for a stage or instance in a process</note>. Even if your coagulated **spirit of vitriol** <term>original: *Spiritus victrioli*; a concentrated acid distilled from sulfate minerals</term> is as red as blood and as beautiful as a **ruby**, it still lacks the most necessary thing: namely the **penetrating flow** and the **resolving saline** <term>original: *resolvirende salinische*; the power of salt-based substances to dissolve other matters</term> nature. Therefore, the conclusion of **Basil Valentine** <note>original: *Basilii*; referring to the legendary 15th-century alchemical authority Basil Valentine, whose writings were highly influential</note> appears to be true: that the spirit departs during the destruction and leaves the dwelling like a tenant, but takes up residence in the gold. From this, one must conclude that nothing can be accomplished in **transmutation** <term>the alchemical process of changing one substance into another</term>, because the red **mass** <term>original: *Massa*</term> is as hard as iron, and the "red coat" <note>An alchemical metaphor for the outer appearance or "garment" of a substance</note> is subject to such a great change that it turns into a black slag in the fire. And even if the **ferment** <term>a substance used to trigger a transformation, much like yeast in bread</term> of gold were present, it is still the work of the ignorant, who know neither the beginning, middle, nor end of this art. And how could a **radical resolution** <term>original: *radicalische Resolution*; a fundamental, deep chemical breakdown</term> and that most noble, true **potable gold** <term>original: *Aurum potabile*; a legendary medicinal elixir of "drinkable gold" believed to grant health and longevity</term> be achieved with this **spirit of vitriol**, when it is an exceedingly great **corrosive** <term>original: *Corrosio*</term> which gnaws and eats away at iron sooner than straw, and causes much damage in the human body? On the other hand, the gold prepared through the true **philosophical way** is as sweet as sugar and is not unjustly called **Sugar of the Sun** <term>original: *Sacharum Solis*; "Solis" refers to the Sun, which is the alchemical symbol for Gold</term>.

<header>Part One /</header>

...water is transmuted into copper because iron and copper ore break <note>meaning they are found or mined in the same veins</note> near each other.

In truth, I wish to report something else, so that it may be clearly seen how **Claveus** <note>Gaston de Claves, a defender of alchemy</note> with this argument against **Erastus** <note>Thomas Erastus, a famous critic of alchemy</note> did too little for the matter and maintained the **foundation of transmutation** very poorly. I myself was once very mistaken in this; **Suchtenius** <note>Alexander von Suchten, a 16th-century alchemist</note> and other authors indeed affirm that copper made from iron is very closely related to gold. For this reason, many **processes** for **"Venus from Mars"** <note>original: *Venere ex Marte*; the alchemical transformation of iron (Mars) into copper (Venus)</note> were sold for great sums of money. This was because such **Venus**, with her hot **sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphur*; the alchemical principle of heat and soul</note>, could easily warm the cold body of the **Moon** <note>original: *Monden*; silver</note> and, according to the opinion of **Theophrastus** <note>Paracelsus</note> and **Korndörffer**, make her equal to her brother the **Sun** <note>original: *Sonnen*; gold</note>, so that the Moon might afterwards drive in a wagon with four wheels just like her brother <note>a metaphorical way of saying the silver would become gold, sharing its "regal" properties</note>.

But in truth, something much different is to be understood by **philosophical brass** <term>original: *ære Philosophico*; a symbolic substance in the alchemical process, distinct from the common metal used by blacksmiths</term> than [common] copper; and the fact that they have not yet understood this **philosophical brass** <note>original: *æs Philosophicum*</note> has deceived a great many of them. Yet, by the grace of God, this error was removed from me in such a way that I took a Hungarian and a common Goslar **vitriol** <note>sulfate minerals from the mining town of Goslar in the Harz mountains</note> and proceeded with them as the **practice** <note>the specific lab instructions</note> of Claveus teaches. There, I obtained considerably more copper from the Hungarian vitriol than from the common one, which I held for a long time to be **transmuted Mars** <note>iron that had been successfully changed into copper</note>; however...

to analyze the <term>caput mortuum</term> <note>original Latin: "dead head"; the useless solid residue remaining after distillation or sublimation</note>,
I noticed a powerful and thought-provoking 
difference in the <term>Substantz</term> <note>the material essence or physical matter</note>.
Therefore, I immediately dissolved one <term>Loth</term> <note>a historical German unit of weight, approximately 14.6 grams or half an ounce</note> 
of pure, refined copper in a water made of vitriol 
and salt; and into this same 
beautiful blue, astringent solution, I placed 2 <term>Loth</term> 
of pure iron sheets and left it 
to stand in the heat. 
Then the iron sheets disappeared, 
and the water—which was 
previously green and sweet—became 
entirely white with a red skin. 
At the bottom <note>original: *in Fundo*</note>, however, lay much 
red copper, as beautiful as if it 
had been filed. 
After rinsing and drying this, I weighed it; 
it was a little more than one <term>Loth</term>. 
When mixed with black flux <note>a mixture of tartar and nitre used in smelting to separate metal from ore</note> 
and smelted, I obtained a full <term>Loth</term> of copper 
without any loss or change. 
I let the water evaporate, 
and a white vitriol crystallized out, 
which crumbled after gentle drying 
and became like a salt; 
it weighed 2 <term>Loth</term> in weight. 
Thus, I recovered my 
copper, which I had used for the test, 
and on the other hand, the iron was calcined <note>reduced to a powdery oxide through heat</note>, over

and it is to be tested / for **vitriol** <note>a historical term for metal sulfates, such as iron or copper sulfate</note> is only salt / therefore salt immediately precipitates silver and gold / indeed all dissolved bodies / because the salt easily dissolves in water / of necessity that which is heavy and fixed must fall down to the bottom.

brought into motion / and what kind of path this was / for while the most artistic furnaces / that human eyes have ever seen / were manufactured / and all sorts of instruments <term>Instrumenta; the specialized glassware and metal tools used in a laboratory</term> were procured at great cost / also a considerable quantity of the purest arsenic <note>original: *Arsenici*</note> was mixed with a beautiful silver calx <term>Silberkalck; a powder or oxide of silver produced by calcination</term> / and digested with a very gentle degree of fire / not only for 18 weeks / but for nearly 4 years without any cessation / yet the wings of the arsenic <note>The "wings" are an alchemical metaphor for volatility; the goal was to "clip the wings" to make the arsenic "fixed" or stable so it would not evaporate under heat</note> were not lost or burned away / rather, one found the worst poison / just as it was in the beginning / and the result was so poor / that one could not bring about the ferment <term>Fermentum; a substance intended to act like a starter or yeast to "ripen" the metal into gold or silver</term> again. Whoever desires to gather from this Philosophical Garden <note>A common alchemical metaphor for the body of hermetic knowledge and the laboratory work itself.</note> should know that our philosophical *subjectum* <note>original: *subjectum*; the starting material or "prime matter" of the work</note> must not have been in any fire; otherwise, one will not obtain the **Bird of Hermes** <note>original: *Vöglein Hermetis*; a symbol for the volatile spirit or mercury that must be captured and "fixed" during the alchemical process</note>. Since this is the case, it can be inferred that the **Vitriol of Mars** <note>original: *Victriolum Martis*; iron sulfate</note> and **Venus** <note>original: *Veneris*; copper sulfate</note>—which have already endured the test of **Vulcan** <note>original: *Vulcani Examen*; "Vulcan" refers to fire; the "test" refers to the smelting or high-heat processing of the metals</note> and have been enticed out of a **dead earth** <note>original: *terra Emortua*; the "caput mortuum" or useless dross left after distillation or sublimation</note>—can achieve nothing useful. Their **living spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus Vivus*</note> is no longer present, having been driven out **by the force of fire** <note>original: *vi ignis*</note>. It would be far better to take up the **Vitriol of the Sun** and **the Moon** <note>original: *Victriolum Solis und Lunæ*; referring to the vitriols of Gold and Silver</note> (if vitriol were to be of any value at all), because they are more closely related to the goal than the others, and to **proceed** with them **philosophically** <note>original: *philosophicè zu Procediren*; to work according to the true principles of alchemy rather than "vulgar" chemistry</note>. It is better to do this than to wait upon the others without benefit and with wasted **expense** <note>original: *Spesa*</note>. But just as the former has a basis in truth, we shall let all the rest remain as a warned-against **wrong path**. If anyone wishes to sneak past it, he may remain lying endlessly in the fetters of error until the Most High allows another judgment to be passed.

<margin>† 3. Wrong path regarding **Saturn** <note>original: *Saturno*; the alchemical name for Lead</note>.</margin>

# First Part

Indeed, is not the **Spirit of Saturn** <term>Spiritus Saturni; usually referring to the acidic spirit or "vinegar" distilled from lead acetate</term> one of the very greatest poisons? Are not all the books full of those who testify that the entire **efficacy** <term>original: *Efficacia*; the power or effectiveness to produce a result</term> is expressly to be attributed to **Saturn** <term>The alchemical name for Lead</term>? If there has ever been a way that showed the beautiful house of the noble virgins <note>An alchemical metaphor for the pure, transformed substance or the hidden "bridal chamber" of the Stone</note> more closely through **reasoned arguments** <term>original: *Rationes*</term>, it is this one; and the most learned **possessors** <term>original: *Possessores*</term> of this **secret** <term>original: *arcani*</term> could find no other means more similar to the truth, nor think of any other way to depict the entire masterpiece, than precisely through **Saturn**.

But so that it does not appear among the **Sons of Hermes** <term>original: *Filiis Hermetis*; a traditional name for alchemists following the legendary Hermes Trismegistus</term> as if they were liars and **sophists** <note>Deceivers or those who use clever but false arguments</note>, they have attached a massive, hundred-weight club <note>A metaphor for a heavy, blunt warning or disclaimer</note> to their writings, in which they give public warning that their **Saturn** is no common lead, but rather the **Philosophical** one. But the Devil, as an enemy of truth, has mixed his stinking urine into the pure milk; whereby all those who have gulped it down have been so befuddled that, contrary to all **philosophical sayings** <term>original: *Dictis*</term>, they have established the **mineral of Saturn** <term>original: *Mineram Saturni*; common lead ore or Galena</term> as the true **subject** <term>original: *Subjectum*</term> of the **Universal** <note>The "Universal Medicine" or the Great Work that transforms all things</note>, and have **defended** it until now, and will not let themselves be talked out of it. However, how true it is, and that they are merely threshing empty straw, I will more clearly **demonstrate** <term>original: *demonstriren*</term> for the sake of the simple and the erring through the following example.

In the beginning, I myself gave belief to the ***Turba*** <note>Referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* (The Assembly of the Philosophers), one of the most famous and ancient Latin alchemical texts</note> and other **philosophers**, and the

### of the Chemical Discourse.

I took the good old **Saturn** <term>Lead</term> in hand in such a way that I looked about for his **ore** <term>original: *Minera*; the raw mineral form of the metal</term>. In obtaining this, I was greatly troubled by speculating due to a lack of the **Philosophical Solvent** <term>original: *Philosophischen Menstrui*; a universal dissolving agent sought by alchemists</term>. Because this solvent was called by countless names, and I—as a beginning student—almost despaired of hitting upon the right one, I fell now upon the **Spirit of the Earth** <term>original: *Spiritum terræ*</term>, now upon **vinegar** <term>original: *acetum*</term>, now upon **mercury** <term>original: *Mercurium*</term>, and now upon **strong water** <term>original: *aq. fort.*; Nitric acid</term>. To my sorrow and loss, I found as much success in one as in the other.

Finally, I remembered the **Philosophical Saying** <term>original: *Dicti*</term>: that our Stone **dissolves** <term>original: *Solvire*</term> by itself. Therefore, as if enraptured by joy, I placed this **ore** <term>original: *Mineram*</term>, well-sealed, into gentle **digestion** <term>The process of heating a substance slowly in a sealed vessel, mimicking the warmth of a womb or the sun</term>, in the hope that the "Old Man" <note>A personification of Saturn or Lead</note> should begin to sweat by himself, bring forth his internal, highly-famed **flowers** <note>In alchemy, "flowers" usually refers to the colored vapors or sublimates that rise during heating</note>, and yield obedience to me as a faithful follower of the **Hermetic Writings** <term>The foundational texts of alchemy attributed to Hermes Trismegistus</term>. But it was in vain; the **Water of Saturn** <term>original: *Wasser Saturni*</term> was frozen much too hard and would not allow itself to be thawed by my heat. I could obtain neither rain nor snow from him, so that I resolved to address the Old Man with greater earnestness through the assistance of **Vulcan** <term>The Roman god of fire; here meaning the furnace</term>. Yet the more I stoked the fire, the less I achieved, and I saw that my labor was spent entirely in vain.

# First Part

Although I was quite burned <note>Likely referring to both physical burns from lab accidents and metaphorical "burns" from financial loss or frustration</note> and held back by this work, I could not forget the **old lame creeper** <note>A common alchemical nickname for Saturn, which represents the metal Lead, due to its slow planetary movement and heavy nature</note>, especially because ***Theophrastus*** <note>Paracelsus (Philippus Aureolus Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim), the famous 16th-century Swiss-German physician and alchemist</note> strongly **recommended** <term>original: *recommandiret*</term> him in his ***Book of Vexations*** <term>original: *libro Vexationum*; a work by Paracelsus concerning the "transmutations" of metals</term>. At that same time, I also obtained several secret writings of a distinguished **Philosopher** from the Netherlands, A. N., through which I was **confirmed** in my **opinion**. Because he—as one who was truly said to have possessed the **Tincture** <term>A potent substance (often the Philosopher's Stone itself) capable of transmuting base metals into gold or healing illnesses</term>—showed in a **discourse** that the **Soul of Lead** <term>original: *Anima Saturni*; the "soul" or essential essence extracted from lead</term> was the true **Gur** <term>An alchemical term for a fatty, fertile "earth-milk" or "primordial slime" found in mines, believed to be the raw material from which metals grow</term> or **prime matter of the metals** <term>original: *prima Metallorum Materia*</term>. He argued that the Tincture must be taken from the metals according to the opinion of ***Theophrastus***, who says: "From, through, and with the metals, our Stone is prepared," etc. He further argued that the **Soul of Lead** was the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <term>original: *Mercurius Philosophorum*; not common quicksilver, but the hidden, life-giving spirit of matter</term> that is called by so many names, and the **Dry Water of the Sages**, in which gold and silver would melt away as if in water; and other such fine **fundamental principles** <term>original: *Fundamenta*</term> were introduced.

After I had also previously found similar **labors** <term>original: *Labores*; experimental processes</term> in old books of recipes—among others, the work of **Brother George from Armenia**, who solemnly swore to have seen a **possibility** of success at his friend's house through the help of the aforementioned **Soul**—I was even more diligent in **preparing** this **Soul** by means of a special water. This substance then had a quite pleasant taste and was as white as snow...

### of the Chemical Discourse.

...proved to be as fluid as wax and very penetrating; I mixed this with the **ferment of Gold** <term>original: *fermento Solis*; literally "ferment of the Sun," referring to gold in alchemical symbolism</term>, and then let it stand **by degrees** <term>original: *per gradus*; meaning gradually increasing the heat over time</term> for half a year. I hurried to the test, heating a little of it on a silver plate. I thought it would still be as fluid as before and would immediately **tinge** <note>In alchemy, to "tinge" is to transform a base metal into gold or silver by applying a small amount of the Tincture</note> the metal. But just as **vitriol** <term>Sulfate minerals, often used as a source of sulfuric acid</term> is as fluid as water at first before its **watery moisture** <term>original: *Phlegma*; the watery, non-active component distilled from a substance</term> has escaped, but afterwards becomes as hard as iron, the same thing happened to me. And when it finally did melt, no **transmutation** <term>The change of one metal into another, specifically into gold or silver</term> occurred. Instead, my **Tincture** <term>The concentrated essence intended to perform the transformation</term> had become dross <note>Waste material or scum separated from metals during smelting</note>, and my **ferment** had mostly vanished in smoke.

It is easy to imagine how this saddened me, but I had to be patient. <meta>This page continues the author's critique of working with common lead. Following his previous mention of the "Soul of Lead," he now admits that his practical efforts to extract silver or a transformative tincture from it have failed, leading him to believe that the famous alchemist Basil Valentine must have been referring to a more "philosophical" or secret form of lead rather than the common metal.</meta>

# First Part /

...had treated this *procedure* of mine in vain; however, a **Running Mercury** <term>original: *Mercurius Currens*; liquid quicksilver</term> had to be made from it with the help of common **Mercury** <term>original: *Mercurii*</term>, and then **coagulated** <note>solidified</note> with the **ferment of Gold and Silver** <term>original: *fermento Solis & Lunæ*</term>. It had to be **calcined** <note>reduced to powder through heat</note> with **potash** <term>original: *Poydasche*; a potassium salt used as a flux</term>, then **reduced** <note>restored to a metallic state</note> and **cupelled** <note>refined in a furnace to separate precious metals from lead</note>, so that a hundredweight might yield a few marks of silver. But how it turned out in the end, and what it yielded, I have experienced all too well; I cannot and will not believe any further that any profitable utility can be brought out of it according to the opinion of **Basil** <note>referring to Basil Valentine, a legendary and highly influential alchemist whose works were often interpreted as both literal and allegorical</note>, no matter how much it is **calcined** and **digested** <note>subjected to gentle, steady heat over time, mimicking natural maturation</note> as one wishes.

One can clearly see, since the **practical work** <term>original: *Practica*</term> will not succeed, that Basil must have meant a different **Lead** <term>original: *Saturnum*</term> than this one. In short, **Common Lead** <term>original: *Saturnus Comm.*</term> has nothing in its "fat" or "salt"—as many others testify—to boast of. Even if one cuts large silver plates and strong yields from it <note>Lead often contains trace amounts of silver, which can be extracted through refining, but the author finds the effort and yield insufficient to prove the alchemical "Soul of Lead"</note>, it still only has one leg, so that it crawls in like a cripple. How could it give and share with another what it lacks itself? Its glass and **Oil** <term>original: *Oleum*</term> are but **smoke and a mask** <term>original: *Fumus* and *fucus*; meaning something that appears significant but has no substance</term>; it vanishes upon the **cupel** <term>original: *Capellen*; a porous dish used in a furnace to refine gold and silver by absorbing base metals</term>. It has no power—no matter how red it may be—to bring **Mercury** <term>original: *Mercurium*</term> into great wealth. Instead, it only **precipitates** <term>original: *præcipitiret*</term> the good Mercury, dressing it in a strange, dusty garment rather than a metallic **body** <term>original: *Corpus*</term>...

<summary>The author describes his failed experiments with common lead, concluding that it lacks the inherent "perfection" required to create a transformative tincture or a wealth of silver. # First Part /

it may have happened that a **philosopher** <note>original: *Philosopho*</note> let the word **Saturn** <note>original: *Saturnus*; the alchemical name for Lead</note> slip, which was then caught by a **sophist** <note>A false philosopher or pretender to the art</note> like a fly by a swallow. This cooled his brain to such an extent that he babbled about it at random, and brought the poor, miserable, wretched devil into such a game that he is treated like a little child: they dress him in red and green, put yellow and black boots on him, and make his old gray beard now yellow, now white. They often milk the **Virgin’s Milk** <term>original: *Lac Virginis*; a milky alchemical distillate or solvent</term> from him, and finally churn the **Devil’s Butter** <term>Likely referring to "Butter of Antimony" or a similar thick, oily chemical precipitate</term> out of him.

But I hope that from my discourse thus far, it will be well understood that nothing can be accomplished with a **sick Saturn** <note>Referring to common, impure, or "dead" commercial lead</note>; for although the blood of old **Saturn** <note>Lead</note> is very closely related to the blood of the **noble virgin** <note>A reference to the pure, transformative metallic soul or "mercury"</note>, the rest is nothing but pure deception and vanity. For just as **Mars** <note>Iron</note> and **Venus** <note>Copper</note> are easily destroyed and accomplish nothing good, so too common **Saturn** <note>Lead</note> cannot protect itself from even the slightest **corruption** <note>Chemical decay or oxidation</note>, much less lead and bring another **body** <note>original: *Corpus*; a metallic substance</note> to **greater-than-perfection** <note>original: *plusquamperfection*; the state of the Philosopher's Stone, which can perfect other metals</note>. 

As for how it is otherwise to be understood—that **Saturn** is the first metal in its **generation** <note>The natural formation of metals in the earth</note>, and that afterwards, through the passage of time, tin, copper, silver, and gold are made from it—I leave that to be **judged** <note>original: *judiciren*</note> by those who, with their sharp sight, have seen through solid stone into the secret workshop of Nature,

# of the Chemical Discourse. #

...and have experienced all such things. I hold that if the **principles of Lead** <term>original: *Principia Saturni*</term> are not mixed at the moment of **union** <note>original: *Conjunction*</note> with the **seed of Silver or Gold** <note>original: *semine Lunæ vel Solis*</note> and **conceived by the womb** <note>original: *von der Matrice concipiret*</note> **at that very moment** <note>original: *in ipso puncto*</note>, then **Lead** <note>original: *Saturnus*</note> can never be brought by the **Archeus** <term>original: *Archæo*; in Paracelsian alchemy, this is the "internal chemist" or formative power of nature that directs growth</term> into such a noble metal. 

Proof of this is seen in the fact that the **Lead** from Villach <note>A mining town in Carinthia, Austria</note> does not contain even a trace of silver, whereas the lead from the Harz <note>A mountain range in northern Germany famous for its silver mines</note> is rich in silver. It is right to ask how this can be: the mine at Villach is just as old as the one in the Harz, and Villach is located in a warmer region than Goslar and Zellerfeld <note>Major mining towns in the Harz region</note>; what then prevents the ascent of this good **Lead** <note>original: *Saturni*</note> to a higher degree of perfection? 

Many will conclude and say: "It is the fault of the seed," or attribute it to impure accidents. In doing so, they judge the **generation of metals** <note>original: *Generationem metallorum*</note> all by the same standard—suggesting that whatever **Iron** <note>original: *Mars*</note> and **Copper** <note>original: *Venus*</note> were created from, gold also comes from that same source, and that they are **at the root** <note>original: *in radice*</note> one and the same. Therefore, they claim it would be possible, if such lesser **metals were purified** <note>original: *Metalla purificiret*</note> and **digested** <note>original: *digeriret*; a process of slow, constant heating to mature a substance</note>, that they could become pure gold. 

But they miss the mark by a wide margin <note>original: *fehlet eines grossen Baurenschuchs*; literally "miss by a large peasant’s shoe"</note>. A hare is no cow, even if it has four feet and sometimes gives milk. These lesser metals do not possess enough power and **perfection** <note>original: *Pervection*</note> that the **Universal Tincture** <term>A mythical substance, also known as the Philosopher's Stone, capable of transmuting base metals into gold and curing all illnesses</term> [could be made] from them...

### First Part ###

...might be tedious to the reader with such tales, because without doubt everyone will wait with longing for what my **aim** <term>original: *Scopus*; the target or goal of the work</term> finally is, and what I am aiming at; so I will break off and remind the reader of this:

# First Part /

...Bernhard himself confesses / that in the supposed **augmentation** <note>original: *Augmento*; the process of increasing the quantity or quality of a metal.</note> with **Mercury** and the **Moon** <note>original: *Luna*; the alchemical term for silver.</note> he found nothing? These aforementioned arguments <note>original: *Rationes*</note> are commonly in use, especially among those who intend to defend good **Mercury** and have not yet experienced its true qualities; and no matter how well-read and experienced one may find himself to be, he is nonetheless liable to be deceived by them and led onto a false, wandering path. # First Part #

...placed my hopes upon **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note>, then I have done so, and let myself be seduced by the lovely, sweet, and seemingly rational conclusions, so much so that I lost all my reason over it and thought I would live and die for it. I devoted myself to **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio*</note> for so long that a good portion of my substance <note>The author likely refers to both his physical health and his financial resources.</note> vanished because of it, and in the end, instead of "something," I achieved nothing.

Here, someone might raise an objection: "Indeed, you cannot be the only one who has completely mastered the Art; perhaps it is to be attributed to your own inexperience that your **labors** <note>original: *Labores*; the physical work of the alchemist at the furnace.</note> all ended so unhappily. The **particular works** <term>Particularia|Alchemical procedures aimed at specific, limited transmutations or medicinal results, rather than the "Universal" goal of the Philosopher's Stone.</term> with **Mercury** require many **prerequisites** <note>original: *Requisita*</note> that you may not have learned. For as often and as much as one takes **Mercury** into hand, it reveals a different virtue <note>In this context, a "virtue" refers to a specific chemical property or reaction.</note> every time, such that one might well conclude it can never be fully mastered. Why, then, should it be rejected and seekers be discouraged from it?"

To this I must respond: I do not claim here to have completely mastered the innate nature of **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii*</note>; rather, I intend only to publicly bring to light how **Mercury** is a "son of disobedience" and how all the prescribed teachings regarding the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <term>Mercurio Philosophorum|The "soul" or essential spirit of matter that alchemists sought, which they claimed was not the same as the liquid metal found in shops.</term> are completely misunderstood when applied to **Common Mercury** <term>Mercurio Comm. (Communis)|Common, store-bought quicksilver.</term>. Although **Claveus** <note>Gaston "Clave" Dulco (c. 1530–1595), a French physician and alchemist who wrote extensively in defense of the art.</note> maintains the foundation of **Chemistry** <note>original: *Chymiæ*</note> through **amalgams** <term>Amalgamata|Alloys formed by mixing mercury with other metals like gold or silver.</term> with very clear words—

# Part One

...Gold as thick as the back of a knife (which some called the **Mirror of Gold** <note>original: *Goldspiegel*</note>); on the inside, however, was a beautiful red powder mixed with a small amount of **quicksilver** <term>Mercurio vivo; literally "living mercury," the standard liquid metal</term>, through which ran entirely pure golden veins, like the lodes in a mine. Furthermore, at that time I saw at the home of a noble man among my friends a piece as red as **cinnabar** <term>Zienober; the red ore of mercury</term>, which was the very best and finest gold; and with the aforementioned work, it was said that it could be **multiplied** <term>augmentiret; the alchemical process of increasing the quantity or potency of the "philosopher's stone" or medicine</term> **infinitely** <note>original: *in infinitum*</note> in a short time and with little effort.

Who was then richer and more joyful than I? I wished to be at peace, and **meanwhile** <note>original: *interim*</note> took both **authors** <note>original: *Authores*</note> frequently in hand, but could find nothing else but that one should allow gold and silver to **putrefy** <term>putresciren; a stage of "rotting" or breaking down a substance, often associated with a black color</term> with **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio*</note>, and **reduce** the bodies into the **First Matter** <term>primam Materiam; the primordial, formless substance from which all things are believed to originate</term>, as **Theophrastus** <note>Referring to the famous alchemist Paracelsus, born Theophrastus von Hohenheim</note> teaches in his **book on resuscitation** <note>original: *lib. de resusc.*; likely the *Liber de Resuscitationibus*</note>; thereafter to **congeal** <term>coaguliren; to solidify from a liquid state</term> it again, **fix** <term>figiren; to make a volatile substance resistant to the heat of fire</term> it, and finally **multiply** <term>augmentiren</term> it. It was briefly stated, but difficult to **perform** <note>original: *praestiren*</note>. [sil]ver or gold / but **Mercury** <term>Mercurius</term> <note>original: *Mercurius*; quicksilver</note> had remained a red powder / I still comforted myself with the **assay** <note>a test to determine the purity of a metal</note> [thinking] that perhaps the powder held the gold hidden within it / but there remained not the slightest bit / I did not know what to do nor how to help the situation / yet the advice was given to me / that I should **fix** <term>fixiren</term> <note>to make a substance stable and non-volatile under heat</note> the work further / [on the theory that] the Mercury had **revived** <term>revivificirt</term> the **ferment** <term>Fermentum</term> <note>a substance believed to transform base metals into gold</note> and made it **volatile** <term>volatile</term> <note>tending to evaporate or "fly away" when heated</note> / a **tincture** <term>Tinctur</term> <note>a potent alchemical medicine or transmuting agent</note> would certainly follow in the end / therefore I remained patient / and **continued** <term>continuirte</term> the **fixation** finally with a stronger degree of fire. I found in the vial only as much of the **fixed body** as the **ferment** <term>Fermentum</term> had contributed / but I had neither a whole nor a half **scruple** <term>Scrupel</term> <note>a small unit of weight, roughly 1.3 grams</note> left from all my bitter toil, expense, and labor / of which I could boast. Nevertheless, I did not give up / but **precipitated** <term>præcipitirte</term> the aforementioned Mercury with a **solution of Gold and Silver** <term>Solution Solis & Lunæ</term> <note>original: *Solution Solis & Lunæ*; in alchemy, the Sun (Sol) represents Gold and the Moon (Luna) represents Silver</note> once again into a beautiful red powder / better in one day / than previously in two years / one part I **sweetened** <note>washed with water to remove corrosive salts</note> / the other I left with the **corrosive** <term>Corrosiv</term>, because the **salt of vitriol** <term>Sal Victrioli</term> <note>sulfate salts, often used as powerful decomposing or fixing agents</note> was said to **fix** <term>fixiren</term> things excellently.    The **Philosophers' Mercury** <term>Mercurius Philosophorum</term> <note>Alchemists distinguished between common quicksilver and a "philosophical" mercury, which was believed to be the true "soul" of metals</note> is quite another thing / for common **Mercury** <term>Mercurius</term> is just as much a metal as gold and silver; just as gold and silver melted together cannot help, give, or take anything from one another / just as much is to be expected from **amalgamations** <term>Amalgamationibus</term> <note>The process of mixing mercury with another metal to form a paste</note>; common **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius Com.*</note> is too weak / to dissolve bodies / and bring them back into their first essence / one also sees it clearly / that **amalgamation** <note>original: *Amalgamatio*</note> does nothing at all except only **corrode** <note>original: *corrodiret*</note> the body / and does not **resolve** <note>original: *resolvirt*; meaning to break a substance down into its primary components</note> it; even when the **Mercury** is driven off by a low fire / the metal of gold or silver remains as beautiful and shiny as it was before / without any loss of its weight / **fixity** <term>fixität</term> <note>The ability of a substance to remain solid and stable under intense heat</note> / or other properties. Whoever heeds this foundation / and believes in **experience** <term>Experientz</term> / will never allow himself to be persuaded / that through the **amalgamation of Gold, Silver, and Common Mercury** <note>original: *Amalgamation Solis, Lunæ & Mercurii Com.*</note> a true **increase** <term>Augmentum</term> <note>The alchemical goal of increasing the quantity of a precious metal</note> / much less a **tincture** <term>Tinctur</term> <note>A substance capable of transforming base metals into gold</note> is to be achieved.

And so I hand over this, my personally collected **jewel** <term>Kleinoth</term> <note>original: "Kleinoth"; referring to a precious treasure or, in this case, his hard-won collection of alchemical knowledge.</note> / to those who thirst for the **pure milk of truth** <note>A biblical allusion to 1 Peter 2:2, used here to describe unadulterated, honest knowledge as opposed to the "sour" lies of charlatans.</note> / and who feel a great loathing for the absurd / groundless, and inexperienced claims of many prominent people / who argue about the **husk** and have never caught sight of the **grain** / nor enjoyed it. # First Part

and would gladly clothe the body of the Moon <note>In alchemy, the Moon (Luna) represents Silver.</note> with the cloak of the Sun <note>The Sun (Sol) represents Gold.</note>, yet know how to find neither the cloak nor its lining. I should also mention how many of them have attempted to make gold and silver **potable** <term>potabilisch</term> <note>original: "potabilisch"; refers to "Aurum Potabile" or drinkable gold, a legendary alchemical medicine.</note>, and devised all sorts of **solvents** <term>Menstrua</term> <note>original: "Menstrua"; substances used to dissolve solids in alchemical operations.</note>, which are yet nothing but pure patchwork; as well as many other **operations** <term>Labores</term> involving talc, **antimony**, cobalt, sulfur, **vitriol**, and the like. However, I have **promised** <note>original Latin-German: "promittiret"</note> above that I would not dwell further on these matters, but rather—as much as can be endured—hasten toward the **goal** <term>Scopo</term> <note>original Latin: "Scopo"; the intended aim or target of the work.</note>. Therefore, I will now aim for it myself, so that my intention—to honor GOD and to benefit my neighbor—may be fulfilled <note>original: "absolvirt"</note>, and that the fruits of my first efforts may be sincerely **communicated** in a **General Discourse**. This is so that it may be a complete work, rather than a fur coat made of many patches stitched together. I will remain with that which God, Nature, and **Vulcan** <note>The Roman god of fire, used here to represent the art of the furnace and chemical transformation.</note> have taught me. 

But I **protest** <note>original: "Protestire"; here used as a formal declaration or warning.</note> against those who have long since worn out their shoes on such paths <note>A metaphor for those who consider themselves seasoned experts but have made no real progress.</note>, [declaring] that this **discourse** is not intended for them, who are already filled with nothing but great **secrets** and arts. For I already know that it is much easier to produce the **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>Lapis Philosophorum</term> <note>The legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting enlightenment.</note> than it is to lead such an old **fool** <term>Narrenkopff</term> <note>literally "fool's head"; a stubborn or deluded person.</note>, drowned in his own **opinions**, onto the path of truth—especially since nothing but truth and simplicity is to be found herein. To those, I **dedicate** <note>original: "dedicire"</note>

<summary>The author dismisses fraudulent alchemical methods, such as "drinkable gold" and various solvents, choosing instead to present his own "General Discourse" based on the teachings of nature and fire. He warns that his simple truths are meant for sincere seekers, not for arrogant "fools" who are set in their ways.</summary>

Regarding the first point / what properties the **Philosophers' Stone** <note>original: "Lapis Philosophorum"</note> possesses / I consider it highly necessary to provide a true and brief report / even if one or another might already know it / yet it depends on this: When he understands this point rightly and clearly / he will find himself at the goal / and can easily judge / whether his treated <term>Material</term> <note>original: "Materia"; the raw substance used at the start of the alchemical process.</note> and imagined <term>Subject</term> <note>original: "Subjectum"; the underlying source or base matter.</note> possess all the necessary <term>Requirements</term> <note>original: "Requisita"</note> / then he will not work so clumsily into the day / and leave things to mere chance. # First Part #

everyone [open] their eyes / and see—whether the **Salts of Tartar** <note>original: "Salia Tartarus"; potassium carbonate, often used as a flux in alchemy.</note> and **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note>, or the aforementioned **Minerals** <note>original: "Mineralia"</note> also penetrate in this way / and mix themselves **radically** <note>original: "radicaliter"; from the Latin 'radix', meaning at the very root or fundamental level.</note> with the bodies.
