# The Chemical Discourse #

...and just as something in the human body divides itself instantly into all the veins and blood / so must it be compared to no other thing in the whole world but a salt or sugar. Our **subject** <term>Subjectum; the raw material or "matter" under investigation</term> must be colder than ice and hotter than fire, if it is to **temper** <note>original: "temperiren"; to balance or bring to a middle state of harmony.</note> the excessively hot bodies of **Iron** <term>Martis; the metal associated with the planet Mars</term> and **Copper** <term>Veneris; the metal associated with the planet Venus</term>, transforming them into the most perfectly proportioned substance of **Gold**; while on the other hand, it must heat the coldest bodies of **Lead** and **Silver** to such an extent that neither of them can ever return to their former state. 

Although to most people it seems incredible that all qualities and **Elements** <term>Elementa; the four classical building blocks of nature—earth, air, fire, and water</term> should come together and be bound as one in a single being, it is not strange to those who see how their **Matter** <term>Materia</term> encompasses everything: how it is water and yet no water; how earth swims upon the water; how earth is water and yet not water; how the air is our **mass** <term>Massa</term>, and how our earth is pure fire—according to the words of **Hermes** <note>referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of alchemy.</note>, where he says: "The wind or the air has carried it in its belly." <note>A famous line from the *Emerald Tablet*, an ancient foundational text of alchemy.</note> 

Finally, in this our most noble **Phoenix-bird** <note>original: "Vogel Phoenice"; the mythical bird of rebirth, here symbolizing the final, perfected stage of the alchemical stone.</note>, all heavenly and earthly powers are present, since it is born from the most volatile and most fixed substances. The Heaven is the father and the Earth is the mother / which reveals itself when the Heaven is **conjoined** <note>original: "conjungiret"; a chemical union often described as a "chymical wedding."</note> with the Earth, and our **Suns** and...

### Part One ###

...the child of the Moon is born. But many will say of this: just as the heaven cannot be brought down to the earth, so too can the **Philosopher’s Stone** <term>Lapis Philosophorum; the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and granting eternal life</term> not be attained. Just as the former is impossible, they claim the latter is a falsehood.

But to answer briefly: we require nothing of the visible heaven or earth, but rather their **forces** <term>Kräffte; the internal virtues or energetic qualities of substances</term>. For just as the **Moon** <note>In alchemy, the Moon often represents silver or the feminine, receptive principle in nature.</note> acts through its forces upon humans, animals, and herbs without being physically present among them, it is the same here. The Most High has placed all celestial forces into our "heaven" and all earthly forces into our "earth," so that nothing further is required, as we shall explain more fully in a later discourse. 

We shall let this brief account suffice as an indication of the properties of the true **Stone of the Ancients** <note>original: "Stein der alten"; referring to the wisdom of the classical alchemists.</note>. Let everyone see whether his **labors** <term>Labores; the practical chemical operations in the laboratory</term> are in accordance with this or not, and whether his **principles** <term>Principia; the fundamental components or starting materials used at the start of the work</term> are gathered such that they are not perhaps impure or useless. For whoever takes good heed of this and **weighs** <note>original: "ponderiret"; from the Latin *ponderare*, meaning to weigh carefully, both physically on a scale and intellectually in the mind.</note> everything well, will be able to have a complete **judgment** <term>Judicium; the ability to discern the truth of the chemical process</term> as to whether he may hope for a desired outcome or not.

Secondly, as to what the **Matter of the Stone** <term>Materia Lapidis; the raw physical substance from which the Stone is created</term> might be: everyone remembers the countless opinions that have been offered on this single point. Some hold that, because this is a **Universal Medicine** <term>Medicin Universalis; a cure-all for both metallic "sickness" (impurity) and human disease</term>, it should and must be in all pla—

<meta>catchword: —ces (from 'Orten')</meta>

<summary>This page argues that alchemy does not require the literal heaven and earth, but rather their internal "forces" placed there by God, and warns the practitioner to judge their materials and labors by these standards. It begins a new section investigating the "Matter of the Stone" and the diverse opinions on what substance constitutes the Universal Medicine.</summary>

<keywords>Philosopher's Stone, celestial forces, alchemy, Materia Lapidis, Universal Medicine, alchemical principles, judgment, labor</keywords>

# of the Chemical Discourse. #

...often found/ and can be made from any thing/ otherwise the name would have been given to her wrongly. Another defends the opposite. Now, this lack of knowledge regarding the **materials** <term>Materien; the raw substances or physical ingredients used in alchemy</term> is one of the very greatest **errors** <note>original: "Irrthümbern"</note>. For if one fails in this point/ one fails/ as is easy to judge/ in all that follow. It is **notorious** <note>original: *Notorium*; well-known or evident</note>, I testify to it also with my conscience/ as often as I have **discussed** <note>original: *discuriret*</note> with various distinguished people/ I have heard in this point from each of them not one and the same/ but rather contrary opinions/ and in all my life I have never been able to perceive/ that any of them halted in the awe of truth; one has **imagined a material** <note>original: *Materiam imaginiret*</note> as he wished/ and **defended** <note>original: *defendiret*</note> the same to the best of his ability/ I have heard so much lately/ especially from the most eminent/ that in this noble Work nothing is named according to their opinion/ much less/ because it is **universal** <note>original: *Universalis*; the alchemical concept of a "Universal Medicine" or solvent that acts on all things</note>/ could one thing be preferred over another.

   Here one sees the great impossibility of attaining this highest **secret** <term>Secreto; from the Latin *secretum*, referring here to the hidden mastery of the alchemical art</term>/ so that one must thoroughly imagine/ that GOD the Most High/ on account of man's ingratitude and evil desire/ lets the reins loose to discord, strife, and quarreling/ so that one might lead the other astray/ as in the time of the building of the **Tower of Babel** <note>original: *Babylonischen Thurn*; a biblical reference to the confusion of languages as divine punishment</note>/ and hinder him from his purpose/

# Part One #

...otherwise they might—if they arrived at the **Philosophical Heaven** <note>a metaphorical state of enlightenment or the successful completion of the alchemical "Great Work"</note>—have the highest **mysteries** <note>original: *Mysteria*</note> **revealed** <note>original: *reveliret*</note> and made known to them. This point is significantly and greatly obscured, yet easy to **resolve** <note>original: *solviren*</note> and difficult to believe; the masters maintain that whoever calls a **Material** <term>Materiam; the physical substance or "First Matter" used in alchemy</term> in this work by name is a **deceiver** <note>original: *Verführer*</note>; even though **Theophrastus** <note>Theophrastus Bombastus von Hohenheim, known as Paracelsus (1493–1541)</note> and others had no hesitation in **naming** <note>original: *nominiren*</note> the **Material** or the **Subject** <term>Subjectum; the underlying substance being acted upon</term> as a **Mineral of the Sun, Moon, and Saturn** <note>original: *Mineram Solis, Lunæ & Saturni*; in alchemical code, these represent Gold, Silver, and Lead</note>. And although they used no circumlocution in this, so many diverse **judgments** <note>original: *Judicia*</note> have been placed upon their writings that many believe **Theophrastus** and **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century Benedictine monk and alchemist</note> never possessed the true **Universal Tincture** <term>Universal Tinctur; the Elixir or Philosopher's Stone capable of transmuting all base metals and curing all diseases</term>.

But such **slanderers** <note>original: *Caluminanten*</note> have never yet been in the Philosophical school, understand nothing of its hidden writings, and therefore cannot **imagine** <note>original: *imaginiren*</note> how one is three, three are five, and five is one, or how two are four, and four are two. They will not admit that this **Secret** <note>original: *Secretum*</note> is a **work of women and child's play** <note>original: *Werck der Weiber / und Spiel der Kinder*; a classic alchemical phrase suggesting that once the secret of the "First Matter" is found, the rest of the process is simple and requires only gentle heat</note>: or that the poor man might attain it as well as the rich. It would be detrimental to their high intellect if a poor, simple man were to prove the deed with his hand before they did, even though GOD distributes His gifts wonderfully.

In short, whoever, without the enlightenment of GOD the Holy Spirit and without diligent work, [tries to open] the door of the mystery with bare...

the greatest potentates and monarchs have used [this] as a means of their **happiness** <note>original: *Felicität*</note> and **dominion** <note>original: *Dominii*</note>. It is a beginning of all things, from which gold and all **metals** <note>original: *Metalla*</note> are born; it is a vapor and a medicine of all medicines, much like a sealing wax—whatever one presses into it, that is what one receives. It is the mother of gold, its powers undisturbed, upon which Nature has worked but little, since the tiny grain of **fixed matter** <note>original: *fixi*</note> within it is still very small. # 101 Second Part

...is the **[prin]cipal and governor of all lights and remaining bodies** <note>original Latin: *ceps & moderator omnium luminum & corporum reliquorum*</note>, and that its **preparation** <note>original: *præparation*</note> must be achieved with a proper **solution**; otherwise, no **tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; a potent medicinal or transformative substance</note> will be obtained. Yet the noble **solvent** <note>original: *Menstruum*; in alchemy, a liquid used to dissolve or extract the essence of a substance</note> remained hidden from them, so that instead of legitimate children, they produced only bastards. Had they learned the unique **matter** <note>original: *Materiam*</note> of gold, they would not have missed the unique **dissolving water** <note>original: *Aquæ solventis*</note> as the first **matter** of the gold itself.

Whoever does not know this can never, in eternity, arrive at a proper **preparation** of gold, but rather it happens as with a locked treasure: he who lacks the key must leave it lying there unrecovered. I myself confess that I had completely despaired over such a "dry **solvent**," but through the grace of the Most High, I received enlightenment to see what the **philosophers** <note>the "natural philosophers" or alchemists of old</note> meant by their dark speeches, wherein I considered what **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus</note> teaches, saying: "If you cannot find your **solvent**, do not despair, but confidently call upon the god **Saturn** <note>original: *Saturnum*; symbolically refers to lead or antimony, often associated with the 'dark' or 'heavy' beginning of the work</note>; he will not leave you without **resolution** <note>original: *Resolution*; the process of breaking down or dissolving a solid</note>, but will give into your hand as an offering a highly glittering **mineral**, which has grown out of the first **matter** of all metals."

If I had not erred so many times in **chemistry** <note>original: *Chymia*</note> and **labored** <note>original: *laboriret*; worked in the laboratory</note> in vain, I would not yet have arrived at this point; for the right, true **Bird of Hermes** <note>original: *Vöglein Hermetis*; a symbol for the volatile spirit or the secret mercury that must be captured and "fixed"</note> is no small thing.

# of the Chymical Discourse

...to find [it] rightly and usefully from a single essence. Therefore, the objection that many have worked with gold in vain does not apply here; one must attribute it to their ignorance and not to the [lack of] possibility. But that gold should have no more powers than what is shown in its physical substance, or that it should not be able to impart anything of its noble blood to its brothers <note>The "brothers" of gold are the "base" metals like lead, tin, or copper, which alchemists believed could be "ennobled."</note>, is not to be believed; otherwise, **Regeneration**, **Multiplication**, and indeed **Transmutation** <note>The three stages of increasing the power and quantity of the alchemical medicine.</note> would be completely and utterly rejected. Just as those cannot be rejected, it is possible to demonstrate that gold is the true **Seed** <note>original Latin: *Semen*</note> of our **Tincture** <note>The "Tincture" or "Stone" is the substance capable of changing base metals into gold.</note>; otherwise, I would gladly see one who would defend and demonstrate another more suitable [substance] to me.

Is it not as clear as day how grossly those have stumbled who sought this most fixed of beings from another **Kingdom** <note>original Latin: *Regno*; refers to the three kingdoms of nature: Mineral, Vegetable, and Animal. The author argues only the Mineral kingdom is appropriate here.</note>, and preferred such to this noble **Body** <note>original Latin: *Corpori*; the physical form of the metal.</note>? Many a man persists with shame and mockery, who has set before himself a very slightly destructible, poisonous, evil essence far removed from the nature of gold, when he should instead have weighed the beautiful **sayings** <note>original Latin: *Dicta*</note> which the **Philosophers** <note>The "Natural Philosophers" or historical alchemists.</note> have written, such as **Sendivogius** <note>Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist.</note> in the 7th treatise:

> Even if you already possessed the **First Matter** <note>original Latin: *Materiam*</note> of the metals according to the Philosophers, it would still be impossible for you to multiply that same **Central Salt** <note>original: *Centralische Saltz*; a theoretical salt believed to be the essential core of all matter.</note> without gold:

## Second Part

> Take pure gold, most select /
> And draw forth its **quintessence** / <term>quintessence: literally the "fifth essence," a highly purified substance extracted from a physical body that contains its spiritual power.</term>
> Without any sharpness or *corrosive*,
> Nor through any deep, sharp water / <note>original: "scharffes Wasser"; usually refers to nitric acid or other strong mineral acids that dissolve metals.</note>
> For where such occurs, it loses its power,
> Therefore use the right mastery /
> Unlock the gold as now reported,
> Use sharp *sal ammoniac* not at all / <note>original: "Salmiac"; ammonium chloride, often used to create *aqua regia* to dissolve gold, which the author here warns against.</note>
> But rather through a hidden fire,
> Thus you shall come to the work most dearly.

And if **Basil Valentine** <note>original: *Basilius*; a legendary 15th-century alchemical authority and author of the "Twelve Keys."</note> had known another **ferment** <note>original Latin: *Fermentum*; a substance added to a base material to "leaven" it and initiate its transformation into gold.</note>, he would have commanded the Purple Mantle in his first Key to be made not from gold, but perhaps from **Mars** or **Venus** <note>Mars and Venus are the alchemical names for Iron and Copper, respectively.</note>; For when one wishes to improve the fruits of the Gold or Silver Tree, no other scion must be taken for grafting than the Solar and Lunar ones <note>Solar and Lunar refer to Gold and Silver.</note>. And although gold alone, as well as all metals, is dead **in itself** <note>original Latin: *per se*</note>, so that it appears as if the **Philosophers** <note>The "Philosophers" are the historical alchemical masters.</note> had rejected it and recommended the Philosophical Gold instead, a faithful follower should learn to see and understand with open eyes that gold is indeed rejected and useless as long as it remains dead—until it is **radically dissolved** <note>original Latin: *radicaliter resolviret*; to be broken down to its most fundamental, "root" state.</note> with the Philosophical Spiritual Gold, **reduced into the first matter** <note>original Latin: *in primam materiam reduciret*; the primal, chaotic substance from which all things are created.</note>, **resuscitated** with its **soul** <note>original Latin: *anima*</note>, **clarified**, and **more than perfected** <note>original Latin: *plusquamperficiret*</note>.

<summary>This page warns against using harsh acids to dissolve gold, advocating instead for a "hidden fire" to extract its essence. It argues that gold is the only proper "seed" for the Great Work, provided it is first "resuscitated" and reduced to its primal state.</summary>

I had hoped my **labors** <note>original Latin: *Labores*</note> would be joyfully rewarded, so that my fellow Christians might also find beneficial comfort in them; however, the wretched Satan—as mentioned at the beginning—brought it to pass that through the harmful state of war, various draggings to and fro, and the final sudden invasion, my substance was devoured, and this noble work was entirely smashed and annihilated. # of the *Chemical Discourse.*

In and of itself it has nothing superfluous about it; it is also entirely like a common **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio*</note> in that it can be driven up into a smoke / which is the life of the metals / and if this loses even the slightest bit in the fire / it is impossible to once again unlock the gold with it and bring it back into its first essence / as the **Turba** <note>original: *Turba*; referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* or "Assembly of the Philosophers," one of the oldest and most influential Latin alchemical texts.</note> testifies / therefore this **ore** <note>original: *Minera*</note> requires in the beginning absolutely no single foreign thing for its **solution** <note>original: *Solution*; the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state</note>, or **separation** <note>original: *Separation*</note>, but only and alone through and in itself / from which afterwards, with the help of **Vulcan** <note>original: *Vulcani*; the Roman god of fire, used here to signify the alchemical furnace or heat</note>, its hot balsam breaks forth / so that it is presented formally / tangibly and intangibly / yet visibly / although one can improve nothing further upon it. Therefore the **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*</note> cry out: Our Stone sublimes and dissolves itself / and does everything by itself; They say / that after the **conjunction** <note>original: *Conjunction*; the union of the male and female or fixed and volatile principles</note> it is the easiest "women's work" / and a "child's game" <note>The phrases "women's work" (*Weiberwerck*) and "child's game" (*Spiel der Kinder*) are traditional alchemical idioms meaning that once the difficult initial stages are complete, the remaining process requires only gentle, steady heat and patience.</note> / and thus at the same time they cast aside all those / who wish to create great subtleties and the elements / and bring about new **principles** <note>original: *Principia*</note> / since nature has already given into the hand of the son of wisdom a **matter** <note>original: *Materiam*</note> / in which the very purest substance / or (so to speak) the **quintessence** <note>original: *Quinta essentia*; the "fifth essence," the purest and most concentrated medicinal force</note> of the elements / and the three very purest **principles** <note>original: *Principia*; likely refers to the "Tria Prima" of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note> are to be found / on the other hand, in the **sophists'** <note>original: *Sophisten*; a term used to describe "puffers" or false alchemists who use deceptive or incorrect methods</note> **separation** there arises a great **doubt** <note>original: *Dubium*</note> / as to what nature would have intended from their supposed principles

...effects / and were terrified by the colors appearing therein before the eyes of all the world / and did not further reflect on other miracles <note>original: *Miraculis*</note>. Many a sensible man / who followed the literal word / stopped where he should have begun; many a one came so close / that he held the Lily <note>original: *Lili*; in alchemy, the lily often symbolizes the white stage of the process (*albedo*), representing purity and the "White Stone."</note> in his hands / recognized its virtue / and yet despaired of its power / which is useful for this work.

This noble white flower is that / which is called by so many names / and is brought about through the art of separation of the pure from the impure <note>original: *puri ab impuro*</note> / but it makes fools of most / in that they / insisting that the King’s bath <note>original: *Bad des Königes*; a symbolic term for the chemical solution or process used to cleanse and dissolve the "King" (representing the soul or gold of the matter).</note> must be pure / were greatly offended by the lowly appearance of the Subject <note>original: *Subjecti*; the raw, base material used at the start of the Great Work.</note> / and could not grasp / that the Most High has covered the little pearl <note>original: *Pernlein*</note> lying within—for the sake of evil men—with such a wretched gray cloak / and the greatest of poisons / and therefore / wanting to be wiser than the Creator Himself / they devised countless solvents <note>original: *Menstrua*; a term for liquid dissolvents, named for their perceived cyclical nature.</note> / so that they might accomplish the said separation / and obtain the true Philosophical Mercury <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> / which Zadith <note>likely referring to the 10th-century alchemist Zadith ben Hamuel.</note> <note>original: *Zachatius*</note> called the Flower of the Sun <note>original: *Florem Solis*</note>: Ripley <note>George Ripley, an influential 15th-century English alchemist.</note> <note>original: *Riplæus*</note> and others called "the seed or the Fifth Essence of our stinking earth" <note>original: *sperma vel Quintam essentiam terræ nostræ fœtidæ*</note>. But because their undertaking was a work of their own invention / it had to come to nothing; nothing foreign must come to our Matter <note>original: *Materia*</note> / as soon as it does / it becomes impure and unfit.

<summary>The text warns alchemists not to be deterred by the "wretched" and "poisonous" appearance of the starting material, which hides a "little pearl" of great value. It criticizes those who use artificial solvents instead of the pure Philosophical Mercury, emphasizing that the work requires no foreign additives.</summary>

In itself, it contains nothing superfluous; it can also be entirely driven off into smoke—just like common mercury <note>original: *Mercurio*</note>—which is the life of the metals. And if even the smallest part of this is lost in the fire, it is impossible to once again open up gold and bring it back to its first essence <note>original: *Wesen*</note> with it, as the *Turba* <note>original: *Turba*; referring to the *Turba Philosophorum*, or "Assembly of the Philosophers," one of the oldest and most influential Latin alchemical texts.</note> testifies. Therefore, this mineral <note>original: *Minera*</note> requires at the beginning no foreign thing at all for its dissolution <note>original: *Solution*</note> or separation <note>original: *Separation*</note>, but only and alone through and in itself. From this, thereafter—with the help of Vulcan <note>original: *Vulcani*; a common alchemical metaphor for the transformative power of fire.</note>—its fiery balsam <note>original: *Balsam*</note> breaks forth, so that it is presented formally—tangibly and intangibly, yet visibly—and nevertheless nothing about it can be further improved. 

Therefore the Philosophers <note>original: *Philosophi*</note> cry out: "Our Stone sublimates <note>original: *sublimiret*; the process of heating a substance until it turns to vapor and then solidifies again, used here to describe the Stone's self-refinement.</note> and dissolves <note>original: *solviret*</note> itself, and does everything of its own accord." They say that after the conjunction <note>original: *Conjunction*; the mystical union of the alchemical opposites, often personified as a King and Queen or Sol and Luna.</note> it is the easiest "women’s work" and "child’s play." By saying this, they cast aside all those who wish to create great subtleties and manipulate the elements <note>original: *Elementa*</note>, and who want to bring about new principles <note>original: *Principia*</note>. For Nature has already placed into the hand of the "Son of Wisdom" a primary matter <note>original: *Materiam*</note> in which the very purest substance—or (so to speak) the Fifth Essence <note>original: *Quinta essentia*; the highest and purest element that transcends the four physical elements.</note> of the elements—and the three very purest principles <note>original: *Principia*; in alchemical theory, these are Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury.</note> are to be found. ...should do? It is an error when one does not wish to distinguish the natures and keep them in order, since **GOD**, for the sake of order, has set every thing in its place so that it might remain there. Therefore, one must feel pity when an old know-it-all <note>original: *Nasenweiser*; a derogatory term for someone who thinks they are more clever than they are.</note> with his opinion has, as it were, banished <note>original: *religiret*</note> so many honest people from the capability of nature, and instead leads them to impossible, difficult things of which he has no basis or understanding.

Many a man boasts that he possesses the philosophical Moon and Sun <note>original: *Lunam* and *Solem*; referring to the alchemical principles of Silver and Gold</note>, yet he can prove such "goldenness" <note>original: *aureitatem*</note> and "silverness" <note>original: *argenteitatem*</note> neither through theory <note>original: *Theoria*</note> nor by the sayings of the Philosophers <note>original: *Philosophorum Dictis*</note>. For according to the opinion of the *Rosary* <note>original: *Rosarii*; referring to the *Rosarium Philosophorum*, a famous 16th-century alchemical text</note>, no Sulfur <note>original: *Sulphur*</note> is to be found under the sun that is like the Sulfur of the Sun and Moon <note>original: *Sulphur Solis & Lunae*</note> (understand this as referring to their fixity <note>original: *fixität*; the ability of a substance to resist the heat of fire without evaporating</note>). 

Therefore, for lack of fixity, no one can call their Sulfur or Mercury <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> an "embryonic" Sulfur or Mercury of the Sun or Moon <note>original: *Sulphur oder Mercurium Solis vel Lunae Embryonatum*</note>, much less "elemental" <note>original: *Elementale*</note>. Rather, he must doubt <note>original: *dubitiren*</note> whether it is not instead an arsenical smoke <note>original: *arsenicalischer Rauch*</note> and a sulfur of realgar <note>original: *realgarischer Sulphur*; realgar is a toxic arsenic sulfide mineral</note>, which is far removed from our red and white ferment <note>original: *Fermento*; a substance added to a base metal to "leaven" it into gold or silver</note>.

This is clearly seen nowadays, in that many take the essence <note>original: *Essentia*</note> now of clay, now of copper, iron, or tartar <note>original: *Weinstein*</note>, or often just a poor, naturally occurring rainwater solvent <note>original: *Menstruum*</note> that has stood upon a gold-ash <note>original: *Goldkalch*; gold that has been reduced to a powder through heat</note>, which extracts <note>original: *extrahiret*</note> nothing and becomes red by itself through the power of putrefaction <note>original: *per se vi putrefactionis*</note>...

# Second Part

### The first key to the **Virgin Castle**
<note>original: *Jungfräulichen Schloß*; an alchemical metaphor for the pure state of matter or the secret goal of the Great Work</note> has been truly found and won, so that nothing more is needed than to humbly call upon the **Almighty** for further success, and to pray that one does not fail during the **Fermentation** <note>the stage where the alchemical agent is matured and its power is multiplied</note>, but rather that everything may be guided and brought to a desired end.

However, this one thing must be well considered: how the **ferment** or **seed** <note>original: *Fermentum oder Semen*</note> is to be prepared, so that the **fat of our earth** <note>original: *Pinguedo terræ nostræ*; refers to the fertile, oily essence extracted from the mineral body</note> may work into it all the better and may quickly **resolve** <note>dissolve or break down into its constituent parts</note>. And because gold is specified for this purpose, it is necessary that it be well broken down and **subtilized** <note>refined into a more delicate or "spiritual" form</note> by the **Fencer’s Bath of Basilius** <note>original: *Fechter Bad Basilii*; a reference to a specific solvent or corrosive process described by the legendary alchemist Basilius Valentine</note>. Otherwise, if one takes gold that has been dissolved by common **strong water** <note>original: *aqua fort*; nitric acid</note> and precipitated by **Salt of Tartar** <note>original: *Sal Tartari*; potassium carbonate</note>, the **Spirits of Vitriol** <note>original: *Spiritus Victrioli*; acidic vapors or sulfuric essence</note> precipitate along with it. This often results in getting two **drams** <note>original: *Quintlein*; a small unit of weight</note> out of one dram of gold; nor will such spirits allow themselves to be washed away or **edulcorated** <note>the process of rinsing a substance to remove acids or salts</note>, but instead they cause significant hindrances.

With silver, one proceeds in the following way: it is dissolved in **strong water**, precipitated in a copper pan, the **calx** <note>the powdery residue left after a metal has been "calcined" or chemically broken down</note> is thoroughly edulcorated, and then it is set aside for use. To be more certain, however, that one does not encounter hindrances because of the **corrosive spirits**, it is more cautious to use **gold and silver leaf** <note>original: *Folia Solis & Lunæ*; literally "Leaves of the Sun and Moon"</note> instead of the dissolved gold and silver...

[placed into] a proper **digestion** <note>original: *digestion*; a process of gentle, continuous heat similar to incubation</note> and **continued** for one month; then one sees afterward how the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Mercurius Philosophorum*; a refined alchemical principle, not common quicksilver</note> works into the **Body** <note>original: *Corpus*</note> and **putrefaction** <note>original: *Putrefaction*; the stage of decay and blackening essential for chemical transformation</note> follows, wherein the entire **matter** <note>original: *Materia*</note> becomes black as ink, and the "Darkness of the Philosophers" comes to light. At that same time, the **calcination** <note>reduction to a powder</note> and **solution** <note>dissolving</note> of the wise masters are accomplished therein, and the **pores** <note>original: *Pori*</note> of our **body** are opened in such a way that, through the help of the "volatile servant," the fixed part is also made volatile, and the child becomes a food for the parents, so that they remain indissolubly united.

The **sublimation** <note>original: *Sublimation*; here meaning a purification or refinement of the substance rather than just turning it into vapor</note> of the wise must not be understood as a thing being driven into the heights and **sublimated** as the **sophists** <note>original: *Sophisten*; a derogatory term for amateur or "false" alchemists who follow recipes literally without understanding the philosophy</note> do. Rather, it means that the dense and **compact body** <note>original: *compactè Corpus*</note> is properly opened and made **subtle** <note>refined or thinned</note>, and the impure is separated from the pure. This is not done by taking **sal ammoniac** <note>original: *Sal Armoniaci*; ammonium chloride salt</note>—as the **alchemists** are accustomed to do—and mixing them together. Although **Sendivogius** <note>Michael Sendivogius, the Polish alchemist cited on the previous page</note> mentions "old urine" <note>a traditional source for distilling sal ammoniac</note>, he intended a far different **salmiac** <note>a specialized alchemical salt</note>; he wished to represent our penetrating **mercury** through such a **salmiac**. For just as common salmiac takes hold and brings about **corruption** <note>breakdown or decay</note> in the lesser metals, so our **salmiac** does to the most fixed **body**: it **corrodes**, **dissolves**, and **corrupts** it, so that a better form and a new birth may come...

### # Second Part

[one] should [govern the process]: *For the corruption of one is the generation of another.* <note>original: *Corruptio enim unius est generatio alterius*; an Aristotelian maxim central to alchemy, meaning that for a new form to arise, the old form must first be destroyed.</note> This is accomplished through the help of an even, subtle fire and through the **Mercurial vapor** or **mineral water**—which is the true **Fountain of Bernard** <note>original: *Fontina Bernhardi*; a reference to the legendary 15th-century alchemist Bernard of Trevisan, symbolizing the secret solvent used to dissolve the philosophical gold.</note>—so that after the end of this degree, the **ferment** <note>original: *Fermentum*; a substance, often compared to yeast, that transforms the nature of the matter</note> can no longer be found, even if the little fountain were completely drained.

If this stage is successfully completed—as can conveniently happen over the course of four weeks—**coagulation** <note>the process of a liquid becoming a solid or semi-solid mass</note> follows afterward. This stage has coagulated and dried out many a man’s brain, so that he lost all reason over it, because he imagined that a "wet spirit" <note>a liquid acid or corrosive</note> had to be used for the **solution** <note>original: *Solution*; the process of dissolving the solid "body" of the metal</note>. Because he saw and concluded that everything depended on the **solution** or the **solvent** <note>original: *Menstruo*; an alchemical term for a dissolving agent, likened to the "menstruum" of nature</note>, he labored now here, now there, to take up unspeakable means for the solution.

As all writings and **books of procedures** <note>original: *Process-Bücher*; manuals containing specific alchemical recipes</note> sufficiently show, he fell into such error and finally did not know how to bring his dissolved bodies to any state of **coagulation**. Even before he attained this, his glass vessels shattered into pieces with a great bang and crash. †. 1. I do not doubt that the faithful **GOD** will, when the right way is known, also grant grace so that the augmentation may happily follow, which, as I hold it, cannot happen in any other way than through its own beginnings; therefore, I will now let the discourse on preparation rest.

The Philosophers say that our Stone is a single material <note>original: *Materia*</note>, and requires a single vessel and a single furnace; thus one must conclude that it truly cannot be otherwise. I myself have had no need for such sublimation, calcination, or reverberatory furnaces, **"Lazy Heinz"** <term>original: *faule Heintzen*; a type of furnace, also known as an athanor, designed to maintain a constant heat for long periods without constant attention</term>, or other various furnaces <term>original: *furnos*</term>. I truly believe that their earthen pots would have absorbed such subtle spirits <term>original: *spiritus*</term> and quickly coagulated them sooner than they expected. # Second Part /

For lack of the glass or solid instruments that could have **coagulated** <note>the process of turning a liquid or spirit into a solid</note> or preserved the mentioned **spirits** <note>volatile substances</note>, all the Philosophers who ever thought of a **dry solvent** <term>original: *Menstrui*; a "menstruum" refers to a solvent used to dissolve or extract substances</term> would have erred most grievously, if another form of dissolution were available. And although some objections might be raised against this, I hold to my own opinion that I may trust my own **experience** <term>original: *Experientz*</term> and the righteous writings of the Sages further than another's imagination. 

Furthermore, the often-mentioned dear ancients, the true **Philosophers**, did not have such great **expenses** <term>original: *Sumptus*</term> to build such beautiful, artificial **furnaces** <term>original: *Oefen*</term> and magnificent **laboratories** <term>original: *Laboratoria*</term> as is common today; rather, they applied themselves to a single small furnace in a quiet place and attended to it. A multitude of many furnaces indicates various labors and many different matters, whereas all the writings unanimously agree that our Art does not consist in the essence of many things, just as **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the foundation of alchemy is attributed</note> clearly speaks of it. 

Therefore, no beginning student needs to worry about many furnaces and laboratories; a single small furnace, in which he can manage his **degrees of heat** <term>original: *Gradus*</term>, is quite enough. Let him place it in a location where there is no cause for concern regarding the danger of fire, so that the Devil—as a hateful enemy—does not raise one alarm after another. # Part Two/ <note>original: *Ander Theil*</note>

[The fire] hidden within the **matter** <term>original: *Materia*</term> (extraordinarily kept secret and named with marvelous names) would do the best; hence the Ancients said: not a **water bath** <note>original: *Balneum Mariæ*; a gentle method of heating using a vessel of water</note>, ash, sand, etc., but rather the **Fire of Quicklime, or the Heat of Sulphur or of our Mercury** <note>original: *Ignis Calcis Vivæ, sive Calor Sulphuris vel Mercurii nostri*</note>, etc., be the true fire. And it is not without cause that these two fires, one without the other, are capable of nothing at all; rather, if an effect of both is to follow simultaneously, the natural fire must ignite the other and make it active. Neither must the cold be taken before the warm, nor the warm before the cold; much less should the external [fire] overcome the internal with too great a force. Otherwise, all labor would be in vain, and the **volatile** <term>original: *flüchtige*; substances that easily evaporate or escape</term> would be driven from the **fixed** <term>original: *fixen*; substances that remain stable in fire</term>, or—because our **mass** <term>original: *Massa*</term> is very fluid—be brought into a state of flow. This has happened to me on various occasions, such that my entire work went to ruin. Instead, the fire must be—as **Bernhardus** <note>likely referring to Bernard of Trevisan, a famous 15th-century alchemist who emphasized gentle, steady heat</note> says—a constant, equal, digesting fire, not too powerful or hot. 

One must not rush the first degree with the **dissolution** <term>original: *Solution*</term>; otherwise, if your **body** <term>original: *Corpus*</term> is not rightly dissolved, no proper or stable **fixation** <term>original: *Fixation*</term> of the volatile can occur. After the **dissolution**, it is necessary that the second degree be conducted, in which the entire mass is transformed into the shape of a white, diamond-like powder; though first, very beautiful colors [will appear] in the glass as well as in the

## Second Part

It is a mere pretense [to claim] he has not used the right **Matter** <term>original: *Materiam*</term>; rather, their **concordance** <term>original: *Concordantz*</term> <note>The "concordance" refers to the agreement between different alchemical authors, suggesting a universal truth beneath their varied symbolic languages.</note> and consistent **discourse** <term>original: *Discurs*</term> has drawn them together in the ground and foundation of truth, so that they may judge whether this or that person is running within the proper boundaries of **science** <term>original: *Wissenschafft*</term>.

## Whether useful **PARTICULARS** <term>original: *PARTICULARIA*</term> <note>In alchemy, "Particulars" refers to specific chemical processes that achieve a limited result—such as transmuting a specific amount of one metal into another—as opposed to the "Universal" work of the Philosopher's Stone, which acts upon all things at once.</note> exist and can be made.

### Third Part ###

...caught and raised by their hands? Nothing at all.
If one of these clumsy idiots were to obtain such a jewel <note>original: *Kleinoth* — here referring to a precious alchemical secret or treasure.</note> and have it in his possession, no greater merchant or jeweler would be found in either Venice or Florence; he would have to be bound with golden chains, and he would make the *Great Works of GOD* <note>original: *Magnalia DEI*</note> so common for the sake of shameful usury that nothing would be left for the *Sons of Doctrine* <note>original: *Filiis Doctrinæ* — a term for the true initiates or worthy students of the alchemical arts.</note>. But because such *particular processes* <note>original: *Particularia* — in alchemy, these are chemical operations that achieve a limited result, such as augmenting a small amount of gold, rather than the "Universal" transformation of all matter.</note> are obtained from dear GOD through His blessing, alongside persistent and diligent prayer, such boasters and dreamers <note>original: *Schnarcher* — literally "snorers," used here to describe those who are spiritually asleep or lazy.</note> must see their understanding of Nature brought to shame.

Here will be found those who have spent much time on the wrong paths I mentioned above, laboring over false processes; those who have lost their possessions through deceitful sophists, and who—having spent two or more ounces <note>original: *Lothen* — a historical German unit of weight, approximately 15-16 grams.</note> of gold to extract it from the mark <note>original: *Marck* — a unit of weight for precious metals.</note>—have departed as fools. They claim that it is all groundless and untrue, and that no particular process can be successfully or profitably put to work, because they have muddied and dabbled their hands through so many hundreds of processes and found nothing—much less been able to bring the *Philosopher’s Stone* <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*</note> to the intended *transmutation* of metals and to *perfect* <note>original: *perficiren* — to complete or bring to its highest state.</note> it. To those I give this well-founded reply: that alongside the true *Universal*, there are also certain...

->of the Chemical Discourse.<-

...know that useful and infallible **particular processes** <term>Particularia</term> <note>In alchemy, a "particular" process refers to a specific chemical operation that achieves a limited result, such as refining or augmenting a specific amount of metal, rather than the "Universal" work of the Philosopher's Stone.</note> are available, which can be practiced without special difficulty. That which occurs in the effect of a whole must also be found in one of its parts; otherwise, the writings of the true **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophorum*</note> would be nothing but groundless. Even if every clumsy **charcoal-burner** <term>Kohlenmörder</term> <note>Literally "coal-murderer," this was a common derogatory term for failed alchemists or "puffers" who wasted vast amounts of fuel and material without understanding the science.</note> does not immediately believe or achieve it, Nature and Truth are not abolished because of him. Rather, just as the **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: *Lapis Philosophorum*</note> has its own special and manifold requirements, so too are these necessary for the **particular processes**.

However, so that every desiring student and erring **alchemist** <term>Alchymist</term> may have a certain foundation—having been warned away from false processes—and so that he may rightly hit upon the beginning, middle, and end of how to reach the most useful **particulars**, I wish to have presented and written the following philosophical **saying** <note>original: *Dictum*</note> for them as a guide, which reads as follows:

->He knows nothing of our art who cannot join and unite gold and silver in such a way that they can never again be separated.<-

In this lies the root and foundation of all true **particular processes**. If my experience is to count and my warning is to bear any fruit, then let everyone learn to understand the aforementioned saying well, and set his hand to the work; thus, alongside industrious diligence, he will find that...

<summary>The author asserts that specific alchemical successes (particularia) are possible and based on the same natural laws as the Great Work. He provides a central "dictum" stating that true alchemical mastery begins with the permanent, inseparable union of gold and silver.</summary>

### Third Part

The veil of ignorance must be pulled from one’s eyes; otherwise, if it remains, everything is in vain, even if one were to devour the very **Mother of the Process** along with its offspring. These are but **sophistries** and profitless tricks of which the **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophis*</note> never dreamed, let alone that they should be the **inventors** <note>original: *Inventores*</note> of such deceptions.

But so that no one further complains about me on account of the brevity of the aforementioned **saying** <note>original: *Dicti*; referring to the quote at the end of page 138.</note>, let him hear what beloved **Antiquity** <note>original: *Antiquität*</note> intends to teach and require with these words. Namely: we should mix gold and silver together so that they cannot be separated again. Is this not spoken of as a **particular process**? <note>As established previously, a "particular" is a specific chemical transformation rather than the creation of the universal Philosopher's Stone.</note> What do you think—if I could unify these two bodies in such a way, what might come of such a **union**? I leave it to you to **judge** <note>original: *Judiciren*</note>; for my own part, I tell you this: such united gold and silver will be impossible to **separate** <note>original: *separiren*</note> in **aqua fortis** <term>Aqua Fort</term> <note>Nitric acid, used to dissolve silver and separate it from gold.</note>, nor in the **quartation** <term>Quart</term> <note>A refining method where gold is alloyed with three parts silver to allow acid to reach the impurities.</note>, nor in the **cement** <term>Cement</term> <note>A dry refining process using heat and corrosive salts to purify gold.</note>, nor by **antimony** <term>Antimonium</term> <note>A semi-metal used in the crucible to "eat" away base metals and leave pure gold.</note>.

Instead, you have the greatest, most useful, and truest **particular**, where your **Moon** <note>original: *Luna*; the alchemical name for silver.</note>—through the help and goodness of the gold—has been **totally** <note>original: *totaliter*</note> **fixed** <term>figiret</term> <note>Rendered resistant to heat and chemical change.</note>, **ennobled** <term>gradiret</term>, and made **weighty** <term>ponderosisch</term>. This is the small piece that the dear Fathers have told you to take out and reduce, for the better maintenance of the Great Work.

->Here the Moon rides on a chariot<-
->with four wheels, just like the Sun;<-

...rested; and the Queen has placed the King’s crown upon her head; the white woman has become a red man, and here heat has overcome cold. From all this, a true <term>Son of Hermes</term> <note>original: *Filius Hermetis*; a dedicated follower of the Hermetic alchemical tradition</note> can well see that the fundamental maxims of the true <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Philosophorum*</note> are of a far different nature than the juggler’s-sack processes of the <term>Sophists</term> <note>In alchemy, "Sophists" were considered charlatans or false practitioners who used chemical tricks rather than following the true laws of nature.</note>. Have the <term>Philosophers</term> not taught: everything that is to be improved must be bettered through something superior, and not through something lesser? How then does the <term>Sophists’ practice</term> <note>original: *Practica*</note> stand? How do they survive with their "fixed" projection powders? They are like butter in the sun: they lack—

141
### Third Part

That they are unable to make any distinction between the **Fixed** and the **Volatile** <note>original: *Fix und Unfix*; "fixed" substances resist the heat of the fire, while "volatile" substances evaporate or flee from it.</note>; if they only understood to what degree of fixity Gold and Silver are set, and, conversely, how their species are constituted for **charging**, **cementing**, **graduating**, <note>original: *eintragen/cementiren gratiren*; technical processes for refining and increasing the purity or color of precious metals.</note> and other matters, they would be ashamed that they—**with all due respect** <note>original: *Salvo honore*</note>—wished to make Gold from filth. However, because the Almighty, according to His merciful **Providence**, reveals such things only to the worthy, I will—to avoid the Divine Curse and the further scorn of **Tantalus** <note>In Greek mythology, Tantalus was punished with eternal hunger and thirst, with food and water always just out of reach; here, it refers to the frustration of the failed alchemist.</note>—write nothing more than what is permitted to me. 

Instead, I have **referred** <note>original: *remittiret*</note> the eager follower to my other writings **on the preparation of the stone** <note>original: *de lapidis præparatione*; referring to the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. I do not doubt that if he draws a correct understanding from those works, he will also easily hit the right **target** <note>original: *Scopum*</note> here, and will know, without further command, how to take out and **reduce** <note>To "reduce" a metal means to restore it to its metallic, usable state from an ore or a chemical compound.</note> something from the united bodies of the **Sun and Moon** <note>original: *Solis & Lunæ*; alchemical terms for Gold and Silver.</note> at the proper time. But in the event that he allows himself to be fooled into obtaining **Particulars** <note>original: *Particularia*; limited alchemical processes that produce a small amount of gold or silver but do not achieve the "Universal" transformation.</note> in any other manner, then regret shall fall upon his neck too late for having so carelessly disregarded my sincere warnings.

When I consider within myself the nature of these **Particulars** and their **Requirements** <note>original: *Requisita*</note>, and contrast them with the intended **procedures** of most alchemists, I am struck with terror that the Devil, out of hatred and a desire to hinder this noble Art, has put such delusions into people's hearts regarding this high Work.

yields; they are even led so far astray that they not only lose their own possessions, time, and health, but also fall into the utmost disgrace and mockery before others. And although their great errors might be somewhat pardoned, provided they recognized them and desisted from them, they nevertheless persist in their stubborn and firmly conceited foolery, and for the most part, believe they are in the right. When, for instance, someone has mistaken a gold-bearing silver for pure **Silver** <note>original: *Luna*; the Moon represents silver in alchemy.</note>, and due to his inexperience has sufficiently tortured it with **Iron** <note>original: *Marte* (Mars)</note>, **Copper** <note>original: *Venere* (Venus)</note>, **Tin** <note>original: *Jove* (Jupiter)</note>, **Mercury**, and **Antimony**, so that he hardly sees a fourth part of his **Silver** again, and it afterwards yields a bit of **Gold** during the parting process—it must immediately be proclaimed as the best **Particular** <note>A "particular" or *particulare* refers to a chemical process that transmutes only a small portion of a base metal into gold, rather than the "universal" transmutation promised by the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, which of its own accord, and with very little special effort or expense, allegedly yields two to three **Loth** <note>A *Loth* was a traditional unit of weight, roughly equivalent to half an ounce.</note> of gold per **Mark** <note>A *Mark* was a unit of weight for precious metals, roughly equivalent to 8 ounces.</note>. But in the end, one hears how that which was brought in "rings"—namely, that nothing remains but the excuse **"I did not think"** <note>original: *non putâram*; a Latin phrase often used to describe the "folly of the amateur" who realizes their mistake too late.</note>, with which they finally excuse themselves.

Whether one should have mercy on such fraudulent **vagabonds** <note>original: *Landstörtzern*; literally "land-strollers," often implying wandering charlatans or swindlers.</note> and still grant them a reward, each person shall judge for themselves by their own emptied **purse** <note>original: *Seckel*</note>.

### Third Part
<meta>catchword: mixer</meta>

I am of the opinion that—since they have fared well with food and drink, the first digit of the 1,000 **Thalers** <note>A standard silver coin; the author suggests their promised fortune has dwindled to nothing.</note> has fallen away, their clothes are singed and burnt, the remaining samples are drunk away in **Water of Life** <note>original: *Aqua Vitæ*; high-proof distilled alcohol or brandy.</note>, and **God’s** punishment already weighs heavy on their necks—it would be a sufficient **Recompense** <note>original: *Recompens*; payment or reward.</note>. Would to **God** that every reckless deceiver might take this writing of mine to heart; surely he would not then **present** <note>original: *præsentiren*</note> himself so thoughtlessly and foolishly before Princes and Lords, but would instead clearly imagine the danger—that such Lords have long arms <note>A reference to the far-reaching power and legal authority of the nobility.</note> and command over life and limb. Such a person deceives himself, for he desires to become wealthy from the Prince, while the Prince, in turn, desires to become wealthy from him; yet both reach into thin air and neither catches a thing. Were the **Practice** <note>original: *Practica*; the actual application of alchemical transmutation.</note> truthful, why would one have such great need for other people’s goods? In such places, nothing is given to anyone before he has **elaborated** <note>original: *elaboriret*; produced or proven in quantity.</note> his claims by the hundredweight; meanwhile, he has no other advantage than that he must baste himself in his own **fat** <note>original: *Faisten*; an idiom meaning to suffer through the consequences of one's own actions or to sweat under heavy labor.</note>. But if he examines it in the light, he has gambled away his freedom for a trifle and, by accepting gifts, has sold himself into the greatest servitude. A more miserable and wretched person is not to be found under the sun than such poor fools; for threshers and craftsmen cease their work at a certain hour, but such a sloppy **mixer of elements**...

<summary>The author mocks the "reckless deceivers" of alchemy who lose their freedom and health trying to trick Princes, arguing that if their gold-making were real, they would not need to beg for patronage. He warns that such frauds inevitably end up in miserable servitude, working harder than common laborers with nothing to show for it.</summary>

[Alche-]mist must wake with worry, grief, and danger day and night, crawl away into hiding, and shy away from everyone, so that the saying of **Job** becomes truly realized in him: *He plows trouble, sows and reaps misfortune.* <note>A paraphrase of Job 4:8: "Even as I have seen, they that plow iniquity, and sow wickedness, reap the same."</note> Indeed, what such a miserable devil has often learned over a lifetime in the noble **Art of Assaying** <note>original: *Probierkunst*; the technical skill of testing the purity and value of metals.</note>, he must reveal all at once without any thanks, while also hearing from many a wicked stable boy that he had known such things better long before him. And even if a tiny spark of truth were to be found in such a person, he would still, in communicating it, have to pour out his best **knacks** <note>original: *Handgrieff*; practical techniques or manual maneuvers.</note> to an unskilled oaf and wait to see whether he is scolded or praised, or whether he receives thanks or ingratitude for it. And even if he had done his best and concealed nothing, no judge could be found who could help him obtain his fair **Recompense** <note>original: *Recompens*; payment or reward.</note>.

Since the work of a lowly **Laborant** <note>original: *Laborant*; here meaning a mere practical worker or "puffer," as opposed to a true philosopher.</note> is such a contemptible business—so that no one gladly looks upon his face, curses his food and drink, consigns him to the devil along with his **Gold-making** <note>original: *Goldmacherey*</note>, and points fingers at him—a righteous and true **Philosopher** <note>original: *Philosophus*</note> should take this all the more to heart and take care to avoid such evil talk, following the examples of the ancients in all things

is to be held as the most hidden **secret** <note>original: *Secretum*</note>.

No one should entertain the thought
that a true **practitioner** <note>original: *Professor*; here meaning one who has mastered and professes the knowledge of the art.</note> of this science
would run to meet a prince or lord and
**present** <note>original: *anpræsentiren*</note> it to him. Those
who do so are doubtful characters; they
desire first to **ex-** <note>continued on the next page as *experimentiren* (to experiment/test).</note>
at other people's expense their **process**...

<summary>This page emphasizes the "Seal of Hermes," warning that an alchemist who reveals secrets for money loses God's favor. It explains that a true master of "augmentation" needs no patron and suggests that those who pitch their services to princes are likely frauds seeking to experiment with other people's money.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the alchemist’s ethical duties, specifically the necessity of silence and the divine protection of alchemical secrets. It follows a warning that those who possess the "augmentation" (the ability to increase the power of the Philosopher's Stone) should not seek worldly fame or the favor of princes, but rather live a life of Christian contemplation.</meta>

### of the Chemical Discourse. 146 ###

[...against further] experimenting, much less that he should seek great friendship and worldly glory. For he is satisfied with little, and finds sufficient glory in the contemplation of the magnificence of his Christianity. Furthermore, he considers that little reliable or lasting friendship is to be expected between himself and a great potentate. For a prince—to whom this noble truth might be revealed as if it were a common or trifling thing—might, in a drunken stupor, thoughtlessly blurt it out <note>original: *auff die Zähne binden*; literally "to tie it to the teeth," an idiom for speaking of something openly or indiscreetly.</note> to anyone. 

Whatever such a man does is done out of pure Christian affection and compassionate mercy; for it is otherwise impossible that any human being can attain such a <term>secret</term> <note>original: *Arcanum*; a deep secret or a hidden "inner" mystery of nature.</note> without the counsel of another true possessor. This remains true even if he were to sew himself into an ox-hide and have himself carried to Apulia <note>This is a reference to a medieval legend or folkloric proverb (often associated with the mythical "Magician Virgil") regarding extreme and bizarre efforts to gain hidden knowledge or travel to forbidden places.</note>. Yet the all-knowing **God** keeps a watchful hand over it and directs all things in such a way that, even if a person of high rank should by chance get hold of this high <term>sanctuary</term> <note>original: *Sacrarium*; a sacred place or a repository of holy things.</note>, he would know neither its beginning, middle, nor end. He would forget the <term>multiplication</term> <note>original: *Augmentation*; the specific alchemical process of increasing the quantity and potency of the Stone.</note> and would be forced to let the entire work slip from his hands unenjoyed, to his greatest sorrow.

From this it can be seen how the dear **God** does not reveal the things He has hidden within nature to everyone. Much less does He wish that such a high <term>mystery</term> <note>original: *Mysterium*</note> be known only to high princely persons, and [towards...]

<sig>K 2</sig>

<summary>This page emphasizes that alchemical secrets are protected by divine providence and should not be shared with impulsive rulers. It argues that the "Arcanum" cannot be obtained through mere effort or status, but is granted by God to those with Christian mercy and the guidance of a true master.</summary>
<keywords>secrecy, Arcanum, princes, divine providence, Augmentation, Sacrarium, Christian charity, alchemical ethics</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the argument from page 146 regarding the accessibility of alchemical secrets. ### Third Part / ###

[...should not] remain closed against the poor, but rather that He <note>God</note> will generously give and grant it to everyone who asks Him from the heart. Both poor and rich, master and servant, should be able to boast of this and enjoy it in the highest stillness with thankful praise to God. Otherwise, it would be impossible for a poor person to obtain the <term>subject</term> <note>original: *Subjectum*; in alchemy, this refers to the "Subject of the Art," the raw material or base matter required to begin the Great Work.</note> or the Art if princes and lords alone possessed this <term>science</term> <note>original: *Scienz*; systematic knowledge or a branch of learning.</note>, and if the <term>subject</term> could only be retrieved from within their principalities and lands. 

God grants His gifts to each person individually and gives them through prayer—yet more so to a poor, humble, and God-resigned person than to a high, proud, and distinguished <term>Pharisee</term> <note>original: *Phariseer*; used here as a biblical metaphor for a hypocrite or someone who values outward status over inner spiritual truth.</note> in whom little fear of God is to be found. Nevertheless, there are still Christian and zealous hearts among <term>potentates</term> <note>original: *Potentaten*; powerful rulers or monarchs.</note> who love God and keep Him before their eyes. It is not to be doubted that—since God, the King of all kings, has honored them so highly and granted them the <term>governance</term> <note>original: *Gubernament*; the office or power of ruling.</note> over His children in this world—He will also not deny them this earthly treasure. 

Therefore, it is better and more feasible that if one—as often happens—must <term>communicate</term> <note>original: *communiciren*; to share, transmit, or impart knowledge or materials.</note> something out of urgent necessity, he should hold to the head rather than the feet <note>A metaphorical expression meaning one should deal directly with the sovereign or person in charge (the head) rather than their subordinates or servants (the feet).</note>. For such a Christian potentate acknowledges such a <term>benefit</term> <note>original: *Beneficium*; a kindness, favor, or gift.</note> with a much higher, more thankful heart and with a greater, fairer <term>respect</term> <note>original: *Respect*; esteem or consideration.</note> than many a distrustful [one...]

<meta>This page continues the discussion from page 147 regarding the dangers of revealing alchemical secrets to those in power. While the previous page began to describe the "governance" of pious rulers, it here transitions into a warning about the treacherous nature of the people surrounding such rulers—specifically courtiers and secretaries—using the experiences of the alchemist Michael Sendivogius as a cautionary tale.</meta>

->of the Chemical Discourse. 148<-

...of malicious flatterers / there are also often many more evil / unfaithful / and malice-filled people to be found among common private individuals / who by far cannot hold a candle in virtue to many Christian <term>potentates</term> <note>original: *Potentaten*; powerful rulers or monarchs.</note>; however, I do not wish to go further into this, but rather give everyone the opinion of <term>Sendivogius</term> <note>original: *Sendivogii*; referring to Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist and diplomat who claimed to have suffered imprisonment and theft at the hands of those seeking his secrets.</note> to consider, where he says: Every time he wanted to communicate something to princes and lords, he fell into the greatest misfortune because of it, etc. And although it is true that truth never allows one to be put to shame, one must not regard the resulting danger too lightly; for even if such things do not happen through the Prince or Lord himself, there are many false <term>courtiers</term> <note>original: *Hoffleute*; individuals who attend a royal court, often depicted in this literature as sycophantic or predatory.</note> present who, as soon as something is achieved, treat such a new servant like a dog entering a foreign kitchen—the others want to bite him and drive him out; thus everyone stabs at such a person, and no one wishes to grant him the Prince's well-deserved <term>favor</term> <note>original: *Gratiam*; grace or favor bestowed by a superior.</note>, especially if he keeps his affairs hidden from the <term>secretaries</term> <note>original: *Secretariis*; high-ranking administrative officials or confidants who managed a ruler's correspondence and secrets.</note> and secret servants, because they always want to enjoy the Art sooner, and the meat faster, than the Master enjoys the broth <note>A metaphor suggesting that subordinates will steal the essence or profit of a discovery before the ruler even sees the result.</note>; if one cannot get along well with these people and let "fox trot against fox" <note>A proverbial expression meaning to meet cunning with cunning, or to act as a "sly fox" among other "foxes."</note>, he had better stay away from the court, however good and gracious the <term>potentate</term> may otherwise be.

<meta>catchword: From</meta>

<summary>This page warns the alchemist against seeking patronage at royal courts, arguing that even if a ruler is virtuous, the jealousy and greed of their courtiers and secretaries pose a deadly threat. It cites the misfortunes of Michael Sendivogius as evidence that sharing secrets with the powerful often leads to ruin.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the dangers inherent in the alchemical pursuit, specifically focusing on the unreliability of people and the spiritual transformation of the successful practitioner. ### Part Three

->of the Chemical Discourse. 149<-

From this faithfully recounted opinion and circumstance, everyone will be able to find how dangerous it is to undertake anything in this matter with any person; for among a thousand, there is hardly a single faithful heart to be found that desires to keep faith and promises. If it comes to pass with one or another that **God** <note>original: *GOtt*; capitalized throughout to emphasize the divine source of alchemical success.</note> deems him worthy, and he may reduce and make use of something from his work, then he will not only be able to dispense with such friendship and communication, but he will forget all hardship, despise temporal and vain things, avoid company, and curse <term>commerce</term> <note>original: *Mercimonia*; refers here to the petty trading and pursuit of worldly profit.</note>. Instead, with previous good deliberation on how he might also bring his <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: *Wissenschafft*; in this context, the secret science of alchemy.</note> to his beloved <term>posterity</term> <note>original: *Posterität*; future generations.</note>, he will long for his end [death] and find himself in an entirely new spirit.

Therefore, let every <term>disciple</term> <note>original: *Discipulus*; student or seeker.</note> of our <term>chaste Diana</term> <note>original: *keusche Diana*; an alchemical metaphor for the pure, silver-like stage of the work or the purified matter itself.</note> be asked to diligently observe that which I have written, to bid farewell to all subtle and over-complicated observations, and rather strive toward how he may wisely prepare the <term>philosophical subject</term> <note>original: *Philosophisches Subjectum*; the primary material or "First Matter" used to begin the Great Work.</note>, maintain it in the <term>philosophical marriage bed</term> <note>original: *Philosophisches Ehebette*; a metaphorical term for the sealed alchemical vessel where the "chemical wedding" or union of opposites occurs.</note> with appropriate <term>heat</term> <note>original: *Calore*; the controlled fire of the furnace.</note>, and learn to await the harvest with <term>patience</term> <note>original: *Gedult*.</note>. In the meantime, let his prayer and patience be firm and constant; do not yield from one perceived possibility to another, but follow Nature, strive to fear **God**, love your neighbor, and be ready at all times—

<summary>The author warns that human nature is too treacherous for sharing alchemical secrets and describes how the successful alchemist eventually turns away from worldly greed toward spiritual peace and concern for future generations. He concludes with practical advice to stay focused on "Nature" and maintain a steady heat in the alchemical vessel while remaining pious.</summary>

<keywords>alchemical secrecy, chaste Diana, philosophical marriage bed, heat, patience, posterity, Sendivogius, spiritual transformation</keywords>

### Part Three

->of the Chemical Discourse. 150<-

to give an account to **GOD** in heaven; let him, on the other hand, guard himself against all those who have no other **foundations** <note>original: *Fundamenta*</note> than what was recounted above; let him take an example from those who propose nothing but absurdities, such as those who hold their spruce and fir resin to be the true **subject** <note>original: *Subjectum*; the raw starting material of the Great Work</note>, and for that reason have some hundreds of Thalers given to them—along with an **oath** <note>original: *Juramento*</note> and a **seal of silence** <note>original: *Sigillo silentii*</note>—just for the naming of it. They do this in the opinion that no closer **matter** <note>original: *Materia*</note> and no other **gum** <note>original: *Gummi*</note> of our **Philosopher's Stone** <note>original: *Philosophischen Steins*</note> exists—simply because the deceptive [divining] rods struck upon it—and they claim that the **Dragon of the Philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophorum Drach*; a common symbol for the volatile or raw mercury</note> lurks within it, and that the fire of the **last judgment** <note>original: *extremi judicii*</note> is depicted thereby. Yet the rod strikes just as well upon common water, and many other **gums** <note>original: *Gummata*</note> are brought from the Morning [Eastern] and Midday [Southern] lands which far surpass common resin. 

He should avoid all large **compositions** <note>original: *Compositiones*; complex recipes involving many ingredients</note>, and allow himself to imagine no **particular** <term>particular</term> <note>A "particular" work in alchemy refers to a process that transmutates only a specific amount of base metal into gold or silver, rather than the "Universal" medicine or Stone.</note>, because common silver can be **improved** <note>original: *melioriret*</note> in no other way than through the goodness of gold. He should consider it a borrowed [worthless] chatter that no gold exists that was not previously silver, and that no silver exists that was not previously lead, tin, or copper; for if this were true, **royal water** <term>Aqua Regis</term> <note>original: *Aqua Regis*; a mixture of acids that dissolves gold.</note> would **dissolve** <note>original: *solviren*</note> silver as well as gold, and common **strong water** <term>Aqua Fortis</term> <note>original: *Aqua Fort*; nitric acid, which dissolves silver but leaves gold intact.</note> would dissolve gold as well as silver. Furthermore, the **particulars** would not be so expensive, for we would not need to look long for a **tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; the substance used to "dye" or transmute base metals into noble ones</note>—

<summary>This page warns the aspiring alchemist against fraudsters who sell common resins as secret ingredients under oaths of secrecy and argues against the theory that metals must evolve sequentially from lead to silver to gold.</summary>

<keywords>fraudulent alchemy, resins, divining rods, particular work, Aqua Regis, Aqua Fortis, metallic transmutation, Dragon of the Philosophers</keywords>

--- Page 175 ---
<meta>This page concludes the third part of the discourse, transitioning from the critique of false alchemists to the author's final theory on metallic generation and a concluding blessing for peace.</meta>

### Third Part

it would be a poor thing if lead, copper, or silver ore were digested and transmuted into a better metal in such a way that such a possibility <note>OCR reads "Possilität," a typo for "Possibilität."</note> could be brought further in the alchemists' furnaces in one year than Nature’s cook <note>A common alchemical metaphor for the geothermal heat within the earth that matures minerals over centuries.</note> in the earth could manage in 1,000 years. I remain, finally, of the opinion that every genus <note>original: *Genus*; type or category of being</note> has its own seed and cannot be improved except through the superfluous fixity <note>The quality of being "fixed," or resistant to the transformative power of fire.</note> of gold and silver. On the other hand, I grant that the beginning of metals is commonly a vitriolic, salt-like water, which is impregnated by the sulfur <note>original: *Sulphure*; in alchemy, Sulfur represents the active, combustible, and masculine principle of a substance.</note> of every kind, and is afterwards transformed into a metal according to what that sulfur is. Now, if someone were to resolve the lesser metals into their first water <note>The "first water" or *primordial water* refers to the original liquid state of matter before it solidified into metal.</note> by means of the first water, and then wished to impregnate and fix them with the seed of gold and silver, one would see the outcome of such labor <note>original: *Laboris*; work or chemical operation</note>, and one could find the first water of the metals. Let everyone take warning and follow my teaching; thus he will perceive that his work is well-applied. I shall then take cause to bring my good thoughts further to light. Meanwhile, may Almighty God grant the favorable reader all good health and happy prosperity, and bestow upon us all the noble and long-desired peace.

-> T H E  E N D . <-

<summary>The author concludes by theorizing that metals originate from a "vitriolic water" impregnated by sulfur and argues that transmutation requires returning metals to this primary liquid state. The text ends with a traditional blessing for the reader's health and a prayer for peace.</summary>

<keywords>metallic seed, transmutation, sulfur, vitriol, first water, fixity, Nature's cook, alchemy, peace</keywords>
